'archive_dateformat'
'Preferences: Archive date format'
<p>How dates will appear on articles in sections of your site.</p>

'archive_date_case'
'Preferences: Subsection date case'
<p>Use this if you want dates for articles in subsections to appear in <span class="caps">UPPERCASE</span>, Upper and lowercase, or lowercase.</p>
<p>Upper and lowercase is the default.</p>

'articles_use_excerpts'
'Preferences: Use excerpts on articles?'
<p>By choosing &apos;No&apos; the excerpt field will not be shown to the author on the article editing screen.</p>

'article_category'
'Categories: Articles'
<p>Unlike sections, which define the navigational structure of a site, article categories allow organization of articles by their subject matter. Once categories are assigned, lists of articles belonging to particular categories can be browsed.</p>
<p>For example, likely article categories on a site about food might be:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>raw ingredients</li>
		<li>shopping</li>
		<li>recipes</li>
		<li>presentation</li>
		<li>restaurants</li>
		<li>chefs</li>
	</ul>

'article_image'
'Write: Article image'
<p>If you would like to associate a single image with this article, place one of the following here:</p>

	<ol>
		<li>a Textpattern image ID</li>
		<li>a relative <span class="caps">URL</span> to the image (e.g. <code>/images/my_pic.jpg</code>)</li>
		<li>a fully-qualified <span class="caps">URL</span> (e.g. <code>https://example.com/path/to/images/my_pic.jpg</code>)</li>
	</ol>

<p>The image can be displayed by placing a <a href="https://docs.textpattern.com/tags/article-image" target="_blank"><code>&lt;txp:article_image /&gt;</code> tag</a> inside an article form. It will output a complete <span class="caps">HTML</span> <code>&lt;img&gt;</code> tag to your chosen image.</p>

'article_template'
'Preferences: Default article template'
<p>This is the default template used to format articles on the front page of your site (provided none has been specified in the <span class="caps">PHP</span> snippet used on that page).</p>

'attach_titles_to_permalinks'
'Preferences: Attach titles to permalinks?'
<p>This setting will attach a automatically-generated version of the article title to the <span class="caps">URL</span>. It is either generated automatically or can be manually overridden in &apos;<span class="caps">URL</span>-only title&apos; when editing an article.</p>
<p>The setting will only have an effect when you are using clean <span class="caps">URL</span>s. For certain languages this may result in long and ugly <span class="caps">URL</span>s, in which case you probably want to set this to &apos;Off&apos;.</p>

'attributes'
'Textile: Attributes'
'<h3>Syntax</h3>

<pre><code>(class)
(#id)
(class#id) can be combined
{style}
[language]
</code></pre>

	<h3>Attributes in phrase elements</h3>

<pre><code>%{color:red}red% → &lt;span style=&quot;color:red&quot;&gt;red&lt;/span&gt;
</code></pre>

<pre><code>%[fr]rouge% → &lt;span lang=&quot;fr&quot;&gt;rouge&lt;/span&gt;
</code></pre>

<pre><code>_(big)red_ → &lt;em class=&quot;big&quot;&gt;red&lt;/em&gt;
</code></pre>

	<h3>Attributes in block elements</h3>

<pre><code>p(bob). A paragraph → &lt;p class=&quot;bob&quot;&gt;A paragraph&lt;/p&gt;
</code></pre>

<pre><code>p{color:#ddd}. A paragraph → &lt;p style=&quot;color:#ddd&quot;&gt;A paragraph&lt;/p&gt;
</code></pre>

<pre><code>p[fr]. A paragraph → &lt;p lang=&quot;fr&quot;&gt;A paragraph&lt;/p&gt;
</code></pre>

	<h3>Block attribute shorthand</h3>

<pre><code>p&lt;. align left
p&gt;. align right
p=. centered
p&lt;&gt;. justified
p(., p((.  left indent 1em, 2em, etc.
p). right indent 1em
</code></pre>

'blockquote'
'Textile: Block quotations'
<p>To make an entire paragraph into a block quotation, place <code>bq.</code> before it.</p>

	<h3>Example</h3>

<pre><code>Any old text.
bq. A block quotation.
Any old text.
</code></pre>

<p>Will become:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;p&gt;Any old text.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;blockquote&gt;A block quotation.&lt;/blockquote&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Any old text.&lt;/p&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>
<p>Any old text.</p>

	<blockquote>
	<p>A block quotation.</p>
	</blockquote>

<p>Any old text.</p>
<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in <code>&lt;p&gt;</code> tags.</p>

	<h3>Textile documentation</h3>

<p><a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-blockquote" target="_blank">More information on block quotations in Textile</a>.</p>

	<h3><span class="caps">HTML</span> documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)</h3>

<p><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/blockquote" target="_blank">The <code>&lt;blockquote&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> element</a></p>

'bold'
'Textile: Strong emphasis'
<p>Use *asterisks* for text to be <strong>strongly emphasised</strong>.</p>
<p>The <code>&lt;strong&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> tag is applied:</p>
<pre><code>*a phrase* → &lt;strong&gt;a phrase&lt;/strong&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>If the text is not meant to have <strong>structural</strong> emphasis, but simply to be styled in <b>bold</b> (e.g. as a design element) use double asterisks:</p>
<pre><code>**a phrase** → &lt;b&gt;a phrase&lt;/b&gt;
</code></pre>

<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> Quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</p>

	<h3>Textile documentation</h3>

<p><a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-strong-and-b" class="link-external">More information on strong and bold text in Textile</a>.</p>

	<h3><span class="caps">HTML</span> documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)</h3>

<p><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/strong" target="_blank">The <code>&lt;strong&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> element</a>.</p>
<p><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/b" target="_blank">The <code>&lt;bold&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> element</a>.</p>

'bulleted'
'Textile: Bulleted lists'
<p>To make a bulleted (point form) list, place each item in its own paragraph, preceded by <code>*</code>.</p>

	<h3>Example</h3>

<pre><code>* A first item
* A second item
** A second item first sub item
** A second item second sub item
* A third item
* A fourth item
</code></pre>

<p>Will become:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;ul&gt;
  &lt;li&gt;A first item&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;li&gt;A second item
  &lt;ul&gt;
    &lt;li&gt;A second item first sub item&lt;/li&gt;
    &lt;li&gt;A second item second sub item&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;/ul&gt;
  &lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;li&gt;A third item&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;li&gt;A fourth item&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>A first item</li>
		<li>A second item
	<ul>
		<li>A second item first sub item</li>
		<li>A second item second sub item</li>
	</ul></li>
		<li>A third item</li>
		<li>A fourth item</li>
	</ul>

<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in <code>&lt;p&gt;</code> tags.</p>

	<h3>Textile documentation</h3>

<p><a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-ul-list" target="_blank">More information on bulleted lists in Textile</a>.</p>

	<h3><span class="caps">HTML</span> documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)</h3>

<p><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/ul" target="_blank">The <code>&lt;ul&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> element</a>.</p>
<p><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/li" target="_blank">The <code>&lt;li&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> element</a>.</p>

'cite'
'Textile: Citations'
<p>Use ??double question marks?? around <cite>citations</cite>, such as the title of a work being cited.</p>
<p>The <code>&lt;cite&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> tag is applied:</p>
<pre><code>Nabokov&#39;s ??Pnin?? → Nabokov&#39;s &lt;cite&gt;Pnin&lt;/cite&gt;
</code></pre>

<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</p>

	<h3>Textile documentation</h3>

<p><a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-cite" target="_blank">More information on citation in Textile</a>.</p>

	<h3><span class="caps">HTML</span> documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)</h3>

'comments_allowable_tags'
'Preferences: Allowable HTML tags'
<p>If you allow commenters to use simple <span class="caps">HTML</span>, these are the tags they may use.</p>

'comments_allow_html'
'Preferences: Comments allow HTML'
<p>Toggles whether or not commenters may use any <span class="caps">HTML</span> tags.</p>
<p>Note that you can limit which specific tags are allowable in the next field.</p>

'comments_are_ol'
'Preferences: Present comments as a numbered list?'
<p>Determines whether article comments will be wrapped with the <span class="caps">HTML</span> <code>&lt;ol&gt;</code> tag and individual comments will be wrapped in the <code>&lt;li&gt;</code> tag (causing comments to be presented as a numbered list), or whether individual comments will be wrapped with the <code>&lt;div&gt;</code> tag instead.</p>
<p>With either value, you can override the tags used on an individual form basis by modifying the <a href="https://docs.textpattern.com/tags/comments" target="_blank"><code>&lt;txp:comments /&gt;</code> tag</a>, found in the <a href="https://www.textpattern.net/wiki/index.php?title=Default_Forms"><code>comments_display</code> form</a>.</p>

'comments_dateformat'
'Preferences: Comments date format'
<p>How comment dates will appear to your site visitors.</p>

'comments_date_case'
'Preferences: Comments date case'
<p>The case of the date displayed for individual comments.</p>

'comments_default_invite'
'Preferences: Default comments invite'
<p>If you allow visitors to comment on your site, this is the default invitation text. For example:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>Comments</li>
		<li>Add a comment</li>
		<li>Feedback</li>
		<li>Annotate</li>
	</ul>

<p>Note that the invitation text can always be changed on a per-article basis.</p>

'comments_disabled_after'
'Preferences: Comments disabled after'
<p>Use this to set the amount of time after which commenting will be turned off for articles (can be set to &apos;Never&apos; to allow indefinite time to comment).</p>

'comments_disallow_images'
'Preferences: Strip user images from comments?'
<p>With this option set to &apos;Yes&apos;, any images included in comments will be stripped out.</p>

'comments_mode'
'Preferences: Comments mode'
<p>If &apos;No popup&apos; is selected (recommended) the current window will be refreshed to display only the current article, with comments beneath.</p>
<p>If &apos;Popup&apos; is selected, a new browser window will open when the comments link is clicked (this uses the <a href="https://www.textpattern.net/wiki/index.php?title=Default_Forms">@popup_comments@ form</a>).</p>

'comments_moderate'
'Preferences: Moderate comments?'
<p>With this set to &apos;Yes&apos;, every new comment will have its visibility turned off until it has been reviewed. You must manually moderate and make each comment visible (a message will be emailed to the article author when a new comment has been received).</p>

'comments_on_default'
'Preferences: Comments on by default?'
<p>If this is set to &apos;Yes&apos;, comments will be automatically enabled for every article published. If set to &apos;No&apos;, you must enable comments on the article &apos;Write&apos; administration panel each time you want to accept comments.</p>
<p>Note that allowing comments can be turned on or off at any time, on a per-article basis.</p>

'comments_require_email'
'Preferences: Comments require user's email address?'
<p>If set to &apos;Yes&apos;, people that want to comment on your article will have to enter an email address in the comment write form. They will be unable to post a comment if they do not supply an email address.</p>

'comments_require_name'
'Preferences: Comments require commenter’s name?'
<p>If set to &apos;Yes&apos;, people that want to comment on your article will have to enter a name in the comment input form. They will not be able to submit a comment if they do not supply a name.</p>

'comments_sendmail'
<p>If set to &apos;Yes&apos;, whenever a new comment is received on an article, a copy will be sent via email to that article&apos;s author.</p>

'comments_use_fat_textile'
'Preferences: Allow more Textile markup in comments?'
<p>When set to &apos;Yes&apos;, comments may contain <a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-sandbox">Textile</a> elements which would otherwise be treated as plain text, such as:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>headings</li>
		<li>ordered and unordered lists</li>
		<li>footnotes</li>
		<li>tables</li>
		<li>code</li>
		<li>extended block syntax (<code>bc..</code>, <code>bq..</code>, etc.)</li>
	</ul>

<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> For security reasons, raw <span class="caps">HTML</span> is transformed into regular text.</p>

'comment_means_site_updated'
'Preferences: New comment means site updated?'
<p>The Textpattern database keeps track of when the most recent change to your site took place. When this setting is set to &apos;Yes&apos;, a new comment will also update this value. This value is used for example for <code>&quot;Send Last-Modified header&quot;</code> functionality.</p>

'comment_nofollow'
'Preferences: Apply rel="nofollow" to comments?'
<p>If set to &apos;Yes&apos;, this will add an additional attribute to the links made in comments. It is intended to be a measure that takes away the incentive for comment spammers and was initiated by a range of search engines and software developers. You can find out more by reading <a href="https://googleblog.blogspot.com/2005/01/preventing-comment-spam.html">Google&apos;s announcement</a>.</p>
<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> This setting does not influence the functionality of your site. It may prevent or diminish the influence of your comments when some search engines rank sites linked to from those comments.</p>

'css_mode'
'Preferences: CSS mode'
'<p class="alert-clock information"><strong>Please note:</strong> As of Textpattern <span class="caps">CMS</span> v4.3.0 the &apos;Automatic <span class="caps">CSS</span> mode&apos; feature has been removed. The information is provided for historical legacy.</p>
<p>The Cascading Style Sheets (<span class="caps">CSS</span>) standard is a slim, powerful language that allows you to centralise site-wide control of content presentation. Everything from fonts and colours to sizes and positions can be governed through <span class="caps">CSS</span>.</p>
<p>Ordinarily <span class="caps">CSS</span> data appears in the <code>&lt;head&gt;</code> section of an <span class="caps">HTML</span> document, or is stored in a text file linked to the document. Modern browsers parse this data in advance of displaying a page, and then cache the data for use on subsequent pages or site visits. As such, <span class="caps">CSS</span> can greatly reduce the download size of web pages: just one of many improvements <span class="caps">CSS</span> formatting offers over older web design methods.</p>
<p>Textpattern <span class="caps">CMS</span> offers two modes of maintaining style sheets for your pages:</p>

	<ol>
		<li>In &apos;Manual <span class="caps">CSS</span>&apos; mode, you can create (or paste in) any style sheet information you like, right in the Textpattern interface. This allows for quick editing of styles without <span class="caps">FTP</span> uploading. Style sheets can be generated from by hand or by <span class="caps">WYSIWYG</span> website editors.</li>
		<li>In &apos;Automatic <span class="caps">CSS</span>&apos; mode, style sheet editing is taken to a sophisticated new level, using an editing interface and organisational method intended to make <span class="caps">CSS</span> parameters more readable. Any existing style sheet can be &apos;poured&apos; into the editing interface and modified indefinitely.</li>
	</ol>

<p>In Automatic mode, <span class="caps">CSS</span> files are classified as either <em>layout</em> or <em>style</em>. Pages on a site can share or combine either type.</p>
<p>Layout files define large structural parameters, such as the number and size of columns on a page, headers and footers, etc.</p>
<p>Style files in turn govern parameters like typography, colour, and the finer methods of positioning (e.g. padding and margins and relationships between objects on a page) not defined in layout files.</p>

'dateformat'
'Preferences: Date format'
<p>This option will affect how article dates appear on the front page of your site only.</p>

'date_case'
'Preferences: Front page date case'
<p>Use this if you want dates for articles on the front page to appear in <span class="caps">UPPERCASE</span>, Upper and lowercase, or lowercase.</p>
<p>Upper and lowercase is the default.</p>

'delete'
'Textile: Deleted text'
<p>Use -hyphens- around text that has been <del>deleted</del>. This is useful in marking changes from one version of a document to the next.</p>
<p>The <code>&lt;del&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> tag is applied:</p>
<pre><code>-a phrase- → &lt;del&gt;a phrase&lt;/del&gt;
</code></pre>

<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> Quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</p>

	<h3>Textile documentation</h3>

<p><a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-ins-and-del" target="_blank">More information on insertions and deletions in Textile</a>.</p>

	<h3><span class="caps">HTML</span> documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)</h3>

<p><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/del" target="_blank">The <code>&lt;del&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> element</a>.</p>

'dev_version_live'
'Diagnostics: Development code on a live server'
<p>You are running in-development files on a live production site which is not recommended. If you know this already and are happy to do so, you may ignore this warning.</p>

'dir_not_writable'
'Diagnostics: Directory is not writable'
<p>To use the file and image upload capabilities, these directories have to be writable by <span class="caps">PHP</span>. Usually this means you have to change permissions so the web server user has write access to the directory. This can be done with your <span class="caps">FTP</span> client, or alternatively on the command line by issuing a <code>chmod 755 /path/to/dir</code> command.</p>

'dns_lookup_fails'
'Diagnostics: DNS fails'
<p>It appears the <span class="caps">URL</span> you specified as the address of your site is unreachable.</p>

'doc_root'
'Preferences: Document root'
<p>This must be the full server path to the document root of your server, regardless of where you have Textpattern installed.</p>
<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> There must be no trailing slash at the end of the path.</p>

	<h3>Examples</h3>

<pre><code>/home/user/docs/html
</code></pre>

<p>or</p>
<pre><code>/home/var/www
</code></pre>

'edit_raw_css_by_default'
'Preferences: Edit raw CSS by default?'
'<p class="alert-clock information"><strong>Please note:</strong> As of Textpattern <span class="caps">CMS</span> v4.3.0 the &apos;Automatic <span class="caps">CSS</span> mode&apos; feature has been removed. The information is provided for historical legacy.</p>
<p>This selects the default method of editing <span class="caps">CSS</span> in the &apos;Styles&apos; page.</p>
<p>If set to &apos;Yes&apos;, you will see one large text-area with all the content of the <span class="caps">CSS</span> by default (&apos;Manual <span class="caps">CSS</span>&apos; mode). If set to &apos;No&apos;, you will see the <span class="caps">CSS</span> editor by default (&apos;Automatic <span class="caps">CSS</span>&apos; mode).</p>

'excerpt'

'expire_logs_after'
'Preferences: Logs expire after how many days?'
<p>When Textpattern logging is enabled, you can specify how long (in days) the logs should be kept. &apos;7&apos; will keep the logs available for seven days.</p>
<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> The logs only get trimmed when you view the &apos;Visitor Logs&apos; administration panel, and will otherwise continue to grow in size.</p>

'file_max_upload_size'
'Preferences: Maximum file size of uploads (in bytes)'
<p>You can restrict the maximum size (in bytes) for file uploads. Note that this value is also limited by the configuration of <span class="caps">PHP</span>. If Textpattern detects that your <span class="caps">PHP</span> configuration limits upload size to a smaller value, it will automatically correct downwards. If you want to allow larger file uploads via upload from the browser, contact your host (the relevant values in <code>php.ini</code> are <code>post_max_size</code>, <code>upload_max_filesize</code> and <code>memory_limit</code>).</p>

<p>These tags are replaced with their corresponding values every time an article is output. </p>

<p>The time associated with the article, in the format specified in the &apos;date format&apos; items on the Texpattern preferences page. If this tag is placed between &apos;permanent link&apos; tags, then the article&apos;s date becomes its clickable permanent link.</p>

<p>When enabled, pages are <acronym title="Gnu Zip"><span class="caps">GZIP</span></acronym> compressed at the server before being sent to the visitor. This can mean faster page loads and reduced bandwidth consumption.</p>

'header'
'Textile: Headings'
'To make an entire paragraph into a heading, place @hn.@ at its beginning, where _n_ is a number from 1 - 6.

h3. Examples

bc. h1. Heading 1

bc. h2. Heading 2

bc. h3. Heading 3

Will become:

bc. <h1>Heading 1</h1>

bc. <h2>Heading 2</h2>

bc. <h3>Heading 3</h3>

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

h1. Heading 1

h2. Heading 2

h3. Heading 3

p(alert-block information). *Note:* Quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.

h3. Textile documentation

h3. HTML documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)

<a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/Heading_Elements" target="_blank">The @h1@ - @h6@ HTML elements</a>.'
<p>To make an entire paragraph into a heading, place <code>hn.</code> at its beginning, where <em>n</em> is a number from 1 &#8211; 6.</p>

</code></pre>

</code></pre>

</code></pre>

</code></pre>

'htaccess_missing'
'Diagnostics: .htaccess file missing'
'You are using a permanent link mode which requires an @.htaccess@ file to be placed in your site root directory. The recommended directives are:

bc. <IfModule mod_rewrite.c>
    RewriteEngine On
    #RewriteBase /relative/web/path/
    RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -f [OR]
    RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -d
    RewriteRule ^(.+) - [PT,L]
    RewriteCond %{REQUEST_URI} !=/favicon.ico
    RewriteRule ^(.*) index.php
    RewriteCond %{HTTP:Authorization}  !^$
    RewriteRule .* - [E=REMOTE_USER:%{HTTP:Authorization}]
</IfModule>'

'Textile: Images'
'To insert an image, put the URL for the image inside exclamation marks.

*Optional:* text that immediately follows the URL in (parentheses) will be used as the @alt@ text for the image (this also adds a @title@ attribute with the same content). Images on the web should always have descriptive @alt@ text for the benefit of readers using non-graphical browsers.

*Optional:* place a colon followed by a URL immediately after the closing @!@ to make the image into a link.

h3. Examples

bc. !https://textpattern.com/hi.png(Hello World)!

Will become:

bc. <img src="https://textpattern.com/hi.png" alt="Textpattern icon" title="Hello World" />

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

!https://textpattern.com/hi.png(Hello World)!

With a link:

bc. !https://textpattern.com/hi.png(Hello World)!:https://textpattern.com

Will become:

bc. <a href="https://textpattern.com"><img src="https://textpattern.com/hi.png" alt="Hello World" title="Hello World" /></a>

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

!https://textpattern.com/hi.png(Hello World)!:https://textpattern.com

h3. Textile documentation

"More information on images in Textile":https://textpattern.com/textile-image.

h3. HTML documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)

"The @<img>@ HTML element":https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/img.

"The @<a>@ HTML element":https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/a.'
<p>To insert an image, put the <span class="caps">URL</span> for the image inside exclamation marks.</p>
<p><strong>Optional:</strong> text that immediately follows the <span class="caps">URL</span> in (parentheses) will be used as the <code>alt</code> text for the image (this also adds a <code>title</code> attribute with the same content). Images on the web should always have descriptive <code>alt</code> text for the benefit of readers using non-graphical browsers.</p>
<p><strong>Optional:</strong> place a colon followed by a <span class="caps">URL</span> immediately after the closing <code>!</code> to make the image into a link.</p>

	<h3>Examples</h3>

<pre><code>!https://textpattern.com/hi.png(Hello World)!
</code></pre>

<p>Will become:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;img src=&quot;https://textpattern.com/hi.png&quot; alt=&quot;Textpattern icon&quot; title=&quot;Hello World&quot; /&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>
<p><img src="https://textpattern.com/hi.png" title="Hello World" alt="Hello World" /></p>
<p>With a link:</p>
<pre><code>!https://textpattern.com/hi.png(Hello World)!:https://textpattern.com
</code></pre>

<p>Will become:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;a href=&quot;https://textpattern.com&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://textpattern.com/hi.png&quot; alt=&quot;Hello World&quot; title=&quot;Hello World&quot; /&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>
<p><a href="https://textpattern.com"><img src="https://textpattern.com/hi.png" title="Hello World" alt="Hello World" /></a></p>

	<h3>Textile documentation</h3>

'intro'
'Textile: Introduction'
'Some of the many ways Textile makes markup easier:

* Replace single and double primes (@'@ and @"@) used as quotation marks with HTML entities for opening and closing quotation marks (@‘’@ and @“”@) in readable text, while leaving untouched the primes required within HTML tags.
* Replace double hyphens (==--==) with an em-dash (—) entity.
* Replace single hyphens surrounded by spaces with an en-dash (–) entity.
* Replace triplets of periods (==...==) with an ellipsis (…) entity.
* Convert many nonstandard characters (ŸúßŠπŒ) to browser-safe entities corresponding to keyboard input.
* Apply block- and phrase-level structural tags automatically and at the discretion of the writer via quick tags.
* Allow the quick creation of simple and complex tables.
* Allow the simple application of @style@, @class@, @id@ and @lang@ attributes to elements.
* Create hyperlinks and insert images via quick tags.
* Define abbreviations via quick tags.
* Convert @(TM)@, @(R)@, and @(C)@ to ™, ®, and ©.
* Convert the letter x to a dimension sign: @2x4@ to 2×4 and @8x10@ to 8×10.'
<p>Some of the many ways Textile makes markup easier:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>Replace single and double primes (<code>&#39;</code> and <code>&quot;</code>) used as quotation marks with <span class="caps">HTML</span> entities for opening and closing quotation marks (<code>‘’</code> and <code>“”</code>) in readable text, while leaving untouched the primes required within <span class="caps">HTML</span> tags.</li>
		<li>Replace double hyphens (--) with an em-dash (—) entity.</li>
		<li>Replace single hyphens surrounded by spaces with an en-dash (–) entity.</li>
		<li>Replace triplets of periods (...) with an ellipsis (…) entity.</li>
		<li>Convert many nonstandard characters (ŸúßŠπŒ) to browser-safe entities corresponding to keyboard input.</li>
		<li>Apply block- and phrase-level structural tags automatically and at the discretion of the writer via quick tags.</li>
		<li>Allow the quick creation of simple and complex tables.</li>
		<li>Allow the simple application of <code>style</code>, <code>class</code>, <code>id</code> and <code>lang</code> attributes to elements.</li>
		<li>Create hyperlinks and insert images via quick tags.</li>
		<li>Define abbreviations via quick tags.</li>
		<li>Convert <code>(TM)</code>, <code>(R)</code>, and <code>(C)</code> to ™, ®, and ©.</li>
		<li>Convert the letter x to a dimension sign: <code>2x4</code> to 2×4 and <code>8x10</code> to 8×10.</li>
	</ul>

'Textile: Links'
'To make a hypertext link, put the link text in =="quotation marks"== followed immediately by a colon and the URL of the link.

*Optional:* text in (parentheses) following the link text, but before the closing quotation mark, will become a @title@ attribute for the link, visible as a tooltip when a cursor is above it.

h3. Example (standard links)

bc. "This is a link (This is a title)":https://textpattern.com

Will become:

bc. <a href="https://textpattern.com" title="This is a title">This is a link</a>

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

"This is a link (This is a title)":https://textpattern.com

h3. Example (aliased links)

Textile v2.0.0 onwards also allows storage of long URLs outside of the text you're working in, and to reference them with aliases of your choosing. Simply store the URL anywhere in the document, such as at the end, using this syntax:

bc. [alias (optional title)]https://url

Each referenced URL must begin on a new line:

bc. This is a "link":example to my website.

So long as this appears elsewhere in the document:

bc. [example]https://example.com/index.html

Will become:

bc. This is a <a href="https://example.com/index.html">link</a> to my website.

h3. Textile documentation

<a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-link" target="_blank">More information on links in Textile</a>.

h3. HTML documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)

<a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/a" target="_blank">The @<a>@ HTML element</a>.'
<p>To make a hypertext link, put the link text in "quotation marks" followed immediately by a colon and the <span class="caps">URL</span> of the link.</p>
<p><strong>Optional:</strong> text in (parentheses) following the link text, but before the closing quotation mark, will become a <code>title</code> attribute for the link, visible as a tooltip when a cursor is above it.</p>

	<h3>Example (standard links)</h3>

<pre><code>&quot;This is a link (This is a title)&quot;:https://textpattern.com
</code></pre>

<p>Will become:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;a href=&quot;https://textpattern.com&quot; title=&quot;This is a title&quot;&gt;This is a link&lt;/a&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>
<p><a href="https://textpattern.com" title="This is a title">This is a link</a></p>

	<h3>Example (aliased links)</h3>

<p>Textile v2.0.0 onwards also allows storage of long <span class="caps">URL</span>s outside of the text you&apos;re working in, and to reference them with aliases of your choosing. Simply store the <span class="caps">URL</span> anywhere in the document, such as at the end, using this syntax:</p>
<pre><code>[alias (optional title)]https://url
</code></pre>

<p>Each referenced <span class="caps">URL</span> must begin on a new line:</p>
<pre><code>This is a &quot;link&quot;:example to my website.
</code></pre>

<p>So long as this appears elsewhere in the document:</p>
<pre><code>[example]https://example.com/index.html
</code></pre>

<p>Will become:</p>
<pre><code>This is a &lt;a href=&quot;https://example.com/index.html&quot;&gt;link&lt;/a&gt; to my website.
</code></pre>

	<h3>Textile documentation</h3>

'numeric'
'Textile: Numeric lists'
'To make a numeric list, place each item in its own paragraph, preceded by @#@.

h3. Example

bc. # A first item
# A second item
## A second item first sub item
## A second item second sub item
# A third item
# A fourth item

Will become:

bc. <ol>
  <li>A first item</li>
  <li>A second item
  <ol>
    <li>A second item first sub item</li>
    <li>A second item second sub item</li>
  </ol>
  </li>
  <li>A third item</li>
  <li>A fourth item</li>
</ol>

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

# A first item
# A second item
## A second item first sub item
## A second item second sub item
# A third item
# A fourth item

p(alert-block information). *Note:* Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in @<p>@ tags.

h3. Textile documentation

<a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-ol-list" target="_blank">More information on numeric lists in Textile</a>.

h3. HTML documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)

<a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/ol" target="_blank">The @<ol>@ HTML element</a>.

<a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/li" target="_blank">The @<li>@ HTML element</a>.'
<p>To make a numeric list, place each item in its own paragraph, preceded by <code>#</code>.</p>

	<h3>Example</h3>

<pre><code># A first item
# A second item
## A second item first sub item
## A second item second sub item
# A third item
# A fourth item
</code></pre>

<p>Will become:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;ol&gt;
  &lt;li&gt;A first item&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;li&gt;A second item
  &lt;ol&gt;
    &lt;li&gt;A second item first sub item&lt;/li&gt;
    &lt;li&gt;A second item second sub item&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;/ol&gt;
  &lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;li&gt;A third item&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;li&gt;A fourth item&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>

	<ol>
		<li>A first item</li>
		<li>A second item
	<ol>
		<li>A second item first sub item</li>
		<li>A second item second sub item</li>
	</ol></li>
		<li>A third item</li>
		<li>A fourth item</li>
	</ol>

<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in <code>&lt;p&gt;</code> tags.</p>

	<h3>Textile documentation</h3>

<p><a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-ol-list" target="_blank">More information on numeric lists in Textile</a>.</p>

	<h3><span class="caps">HTML</span> documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)</h3>

<p><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/ol" target="_blank">The <code>&lt;ol&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> element</a>.</p>
<p><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/li" target="_blank">The <code>&lt;li&gt;</code> <span class="caps">HTML</span> element</a>.</p>

'override_emailcharset'
'Preferences: Use ISO-8859-1 encoding in emails (default is UTF-8)?'
'Textpattern uses UTF-8 encoding wherever possible by default, including for outgoing emails. However, some older email clients (including some versions of Microsoft Outlook) do not have proper UTF-8 support. If you encounter garbled characters in emails, this setting will convert text to ISO-8859-1 encoding before sending them.

p(alert-block information). *Note:* If you are using a language the characters of which cannot be represented in ISO-8859-1, this setting will unfortunately not help you. You should try finding a UTF-8 (or Unicode) compatible email client.'

However, some older email clients (including some versions of Microsoft Outlook) do not have proper <span class="caps">UTF</span>-8 support. If you encounter garbled characters in emails, this setting will convert text to <span class="caps">ISO</span>-8859-1 encoding before sending them.</p>
<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> If you are using a language the characters of which cannot be represented in <span class="caps">ISO</span>-8859-1, this setting will unfortunately not help you. 'permlink_mode'
'Preferences: URL pattern'
'This defines the method by which individual articles are reached via the URL displayed using the <a href="https://docs.textpattern.com/tags/permlink">@<txp:permlink>@ tag</a>.

'?=messy' mode is the most compatible - it will work with little to no configuration required - but least beneficial in terms of URL readability and search engine optimization (SEO). In this mode URLs for articles on your site would look like this:

bc. https://example.com/index.php?id=123

All other modes require the Apache @mod_rewrite@ module to be installed and enabled on your server. If your server does not support @mod_rewrite@ either contact your web host or use the '?=messy' mode outlined above.'
<p>This defines the method by which individual articles are reached via the <span class="caps">URL</span> displayed using the <a href="https://docs.textpattern.com/tags/permlink"><code>&lt;txp:permlink&gt;</code> tag</a>.</p>
<p>&apos;?=messy&apos; mode is the most compatible &#8211; it will work with little to no configuration required &#8211; but least beneficial in terms of <span class="caps">URL</span> readability and search engine optimization (<span class="caps">SEO</span>). In this mode <span class="caps">URL</span>s for articles on your site would look like this:</p>
<pre><code>https://example.com/index.php?id=123
</code></pre>

<p>All other modes require the Apache <code>mod_rewrite</code> module to be installed and enabled on your server. If your server does not support <code>mod_rewrite</code> either contact your web host or use the &apos;?=messy&apos; mode outlined above.</p>

'production_status'
'Preferences: Production status'
'A Textpattern site has one of three modes:

# 'Debugging'
# 'Testing'
# 'Live'

h3. Debugging mode

All PHP error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins. In addition, the following information will be visible when you view the HTML source of live pages:

* the time it took the server to build the page ('Runtime')
* the number of MySQL queries executed
* approximate peak memory usage
* all Textpattern tags encountered while building the page (also known as a tag trace)

h3. Testing mode

All PHP error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins. The following information will be visible when you view the HTML source of live pages:

* the time it took the server to build the page ('Runtime')
* the number of MySQL queries executed
* approximate peak memory usage

h3. Live mode

No error reporting or diagnostic information will be sent to the page. Generally, Live mode is preferred, as it provides the best user experience and imposes the least overhead on the server.'
<p>A Textpattern site has one of three modes:</p>

	<ol>
		<li>&apos;Debugging&apos;</li>
		<li>&apos;Testing&apos;</li>
		<li>&apos;Live&apos;</li>
	</ol>

	<h3>Debugging mode</h3>

<p>All <span class="caps">PHP</span> error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins. In addition, the following information will be visible when you view the <span class="caps">HTML</span> source of live pages:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>the time it took the server to build the page (&apos;Runtime&apos;)</li>
		<li>the number of MySQL queries executed</li>
		<li>approximate peak memory usage</li>
		<li>all Textpattern tags encountered while building the page (also known as a tag trace)</li>
	</ul>

	<h3>Testing mode</h3>

<p>All <span class="caps">PHP</span> error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins. The following information will be visible when you view the <span class="caps">HTML</span> source of live pages:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>the time it took the server to build the page (&apos;Runtime&apos;)</li>
		<li>the number of MySQL queries executed</li>
		<li>approximate peak memory usage</li>
	</ul>

	<h3>Live mode</h3>

'section_category'
'Sections: Introduction'
'Sections in Textpattern are best thought of as analogous to sections in a newspaper; each belong to the same publication, but each might feature a different layout, or a different style of article, and so on.

For most websites, only a bare minimum of sections need be created.

*A likely scenario for a website with three sections:*

# *Archive:* The 'Section' to which all articles that appear on the front page belong. Whenever a reader clicks on an article's permanent link, it will be displayed in the page layout assigned to this 'Section'. To go to a paged list of the most recent articles assigned to the section "Archive", the URL would be @https://example.com/archive/@.
# *About:* An 'about this site' page, which differs from the front page and the "Archive" section, in that it is more static, may have a different layout, and will likely contain only one article. The "About" section would be reached at @https://example.com/about/@.
# *Photos:* A 'Section' whose differing layout (and possibly different CSS) would display photos instead of articles.

Note that a special hidden section, named default, always exists. This default section uses the page named default and the style called default, and it constructs the front page of your site.'
<p>Sections in Textpattern are best thought of as analogous to sections in a newspaper; each belong to the same publication, but each might feature a different layout, or a different style of article, and so on.</p>
<p>For most websites, only a bare minimum of sections need be created.</p>
<p><strong>A likely scenario for a website with three sections:</strong></p>

	<ol>
		<li><strong>Archive:</strong> The &apos;Section&apos; to which all articles that appear on the front page belong. Whenever a reader clicks on an article&apos;s permanent link, it will be displayed in the page layout assigned to this &apos;Section&apos;. To go to a paged list of the most recent articles assigned to the section &quot;Archive&quot;, the <span class="caps">URL</span> would be <code>https://example.com/archive/</code>.</li>
		<li><strong>About:</strong> An &apos;about this site&apos; page, which differs from the front page and the &quot;Archive&quot; section, in that it is more static, may have a different layout, and will likely contain only one article. The &quot;About&quot; section would be reached at <code>https://example.com/about/</code>.</li>
		<li><strong>Photos:</strong> A &apos;Section&apos; whose differing layout (and possibly different <span class="caps">CSS</span>) would display photos instead of articles.</li>
	</ol>

<p>Note that a special hidden section, named default, always exists. This default section uses the page named default and the style called default, and it constructs the front page of your site.</p>

'send_lastmod'
'Preferences: Send "Last-Modified" Header?'
'When set, Textpattern will read a visitor's HTTP @If-Modified-Since@ header (if one exists) and compare it to the last site update. If nothing has changed since the visitor last loaded the page (i.e. if the timestamp the browser sends is the same as the last site update), then a header is sent back instructing the visitor's browser to use its cached version of the page.

This can reduce bandwidth consumption, page load times, and web server load.'
<p>When set, Textpattern will read a visitor&apos;s <span class="caps">HTTP</span> <code>If-Modified-Since</code> header (if one exists) and compare it to the last site update. If nothing has changed since the visitor last loaded the page (i.e. if the timestamp the browser sends is the same as the last site update), then a header is sent back instructing the visitor&apos;s browser to use its cached version of the page.</p>
<p>This can reduce bandwidth consumption, page load times, and web server load.</p>

'spam_blacklists'
'Preferences: Spam blocklists (comma-separated)'
'The "blocklist":https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacklist providers entered here will be queried when a comment is posted to your blog, and the IP address of the comment poster will be checked. If the IP address is listed it means there has been one or more spamming reports made about this IP in the past, and the comment will be rejected.

Additionally, checks on approved comments will take place when an article is viewed on the front-side. If you encounter slowness when displaying articles with comments, consider disabling spam blocklists by removing the blocklist address(es).

You can enter a list of domain names here, separated by commas. Potential blocklists can be found via "dmoz":https://www.dmoz.org/Computers/Internet/E-mail/Spam/Blacklists/List_of_Major_Blacklists/, but please note that it is your responsibility to ensure blocklists are fit for purpose.'
<p>The <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacklist">blocklist</a> providers entered here will be queried when a comment is posted to your blog, and the IP address of the comment poster will be checked. If the IP address is listed it means there has been one or more spamming reports made about this IP in the past, and the comment will be rejected.</p>
<p>Additionally, checks on approved comments will take place when an article is viewed on the front-side. If you encounter slowness when displaying articles with comments, consider disabling spam blocklists by removing the blocklist address(es).</p>
<p>You can enter a list of domain names here, separated by commas. Potential blocklists can be found via <a href="https://www.dmoz.org/Computers/Internet/E-mail/Spam/Blacklists/List_of_Major_Blacklists/">dmoz</a>, but please note that it is your responsibility to ensure blocklists are fit for purpose.</p>

'Sets an *absolute path* to a temporary (sometimes know as 'tmp' or 'temp') directory. PHP needs to have write access to this directory. This directory is used for temporary files, for example uploads of any kind (plugins, files or images).

p(alert-block information). *Note:* Do not specify the same directory for both 'temporary' and 'plugin cache', as this will cause problems (Textpattern would try to run the contents of temporary log files as plugins).'

<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> Do not specify the same directory for both &apos;temporary&apos; and &apos;plugin cache&apos;, as this will cause problems (Textpattern would try to run the contents of temporary log files as plugins).</p>

'Many content management systems offer trackbacks to individual articles or postings. 

At their simplest, trackbacks offer a way to say, "I've said something about this article; here's the name of my site, a link to the mention, and a brief excerpt". The other site's owner can then use the trackback information however she wishes.

Some have likened trackbacks to 'referrers you insert manually', but that's a little reductive.'

'upload_thumbnail'
'Images: Upload thumbnail'
'Full size images can be assigned their own thumbnails. These are useful for linking to - and the indexing of - full size images.

A reference to the thumbnail is exactly the same as the reference to the full size image, with @t@ appended to the file name. For example, to reference a thumbnail associated with a full size image stored at @/images/12.jpg@ you would use @/images/12t.jpg@.'
<p>Full size images can be assigned their own thumbnails. These are useful for linking to &#8211; and the indexing of &#8211; full size images.</p>
<p>A reference to the thumbnail is exactly the same as the reference to the full size image, with <code>t</code> appended to the file name. For example, to reference a thumbnail associated with a full size image stored at <code>/images/12.jpg</code> you would use <code>/images/12t.jpg</code>.</p>

'url_mode'
'Preferences: URL mode'
'Governs how permanent links to archived articles will appear.

'?=messy' mode is the most straightforward; it will work without you having to do much configuration. In this mode URLs for archived articles on your site would look like this:

bc. https://example.com/index.php?id=123

'/clean/' mode is a little trickier, but it makes URLs that are elegant and easier to remember. Here are the steps to make /clean/ mode work (you must also have 'Use sections' set in site preferences):

Your site must be on a Unix-based server running Apache, or on a Windows server running PHP as an Apache module.

For every section you have on your site (including the default section called 'article'), place a text file with the exact same name in your root web directory, containing the following bit of code, replacing @SECTION_NAME@ with the section's actual name:

bc. <?php
  include "./textpattern/config.php";
  $s = "SECTION_NAME";
  include $txpcfg["txpath"]."/publish.php";
  textpattern();
?>

Next, with a text editor create or open an existing file in your web root called @.htaccess@ and paste the following inside (again, replace @SECTION_NAME@ with the section's actual name):

bc. <Files SECTION_NAME>
  ForceType application/x-httpd-php
</Files>

Once you select '/clean/' url mode in preferences, your permanent link URLs will look more familiar, for example @https://example.com/article/123/@.'
<p>Governs how permanent links to archived articles will appear.</p>
<p>&apos;?=messy&apos; mode is the most straightforward; it will work without you having to do much configuration. In this mode <span class="caps">URL</span>s for archived articles on your site would look like this:</p>
<pre><code>https://example.com/index.php?id=123
</code></pre>

<p>&apos;/clean/&apos; mode is a little trickier, but it makes <span class="caps">URL</span>s that are elegant and easier to remember. Here are the steps to make /clean/ mode work (you must also have &apos;Use sections&apos; set in site preferences):</p>
<p>Your site must be on a Unix-based server running Apache, or on a Windows server running <span class="caps">PHP</span> as an Apache module.</p>
<p>For every section you have on your site (including the default section called &apos;article&apos;), place a text file with the exact same name in your root web directory, containing the following bit of code, replacing <code>SECTION_NAME</code> with the section&apos;s actual name:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;?php
  include &quot;./textpattern/config.php&quot;;
  $s = &quot;SECTION_NAME&quot;;
  include $txpcfg[&quot;txpath&quot;].&quot;/publish.php&quot;;
  textpattern();
?&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>Next, with a text editor create or open an existing file in your web root called <code>.htaccess</code> and paste the following inside (again, replace <code>SECTION_NAME</code> with the section&apos;s actual name):</p>
<pre><code>&lt;Files SECTION_NAME&gt;
  ForceType application/x-httpd-php
&lt;/Files&gt;
</code></pre>

<p>Once you select &apos;/clean/&apos; url mode in preferences, your permanent link <span class="caps">URL</span>s will look more familiar, for example <code>https://example.com/article/123/</code>.</p>

'use_textile'
'Preferences: Use Textile?'
'"Textile":https://textpattern.com/textile-sandbox is a utility intended to simplify the conversion of plain text to HTML. When enabled, Textile will process the body text of articles and return structurally sound HTML.

Textile also offers a powerful but easy-to-learn set of commands with which to format text, define structures such as lists, headers and blockquotes, and insert links and images.

The setting you choose here becomes the default for excerpt and body markup on the 'Write' administration panel. You can choose to allow complete Textile processing, to convert line breaks into HTML paragraph markers or not convert any text at all.'
<p><a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-sandbox">Textile</a> is a utility intended to simplify the conversion of plain text to <span class="caps">HTML</span>. When enabled, Textile will process the body text of articles and return structurally sound <span class="caps">HTML</span>.</p>
<p>Textile also offers a powerful but easy-to-learn set of commands with which to format text, define structures such as lists, headers and blockquotes, and insert links and images.</p>
<p>The setting you choose here becomes the default for excerpt and body markup on the &apos;Write&apos; administration panel. You can choose to allow complete Textile processing, to convert line breaks into <span class="caps">HTML</span> paragraph markers or not convert any text at all.</p>

'using_sections'
'Sections: Usage'
''Sections' in Textpattern are significant in that they don't merely imply a type to which articles belong. Each 'Section' has attached to it a layout (either created by you or generated by Textpattern), a 'Style' in the form of a CSS(Cascading Style Sheet) file, and a physical location on the webserver.

When posting new articles, all this information, which is pertinent to the future display of the article, is attached through the choice of 'Section'.

That said, the first 'Section' we should address is one that can't be attached to articles at all, called *default*.

For a simple website, a single 'Section' (called 'articles', for example) may be all that's required.'
<p>&apos;Sections&apos; in Textpattern are significant in that they don&apos;t merely imply a type to which articles belong. Each &apos;Section&apos; has attached to it a layout (either created by you or generated by Textpattern), a &apos;Style&apos; in the form of a <acronym title="Cascading Style Sheet"><span class="caps">CSS</span></acronym> file, and a physical location on the webserver.</p>
<p>When posting new articles, all this information, which is pertinent to the future display of the article, is attached through the choice of &apos;Section&apos;.</p>
<p>That said, the first &apos;Section&apos; we should address is one that can&apos;t be attached to articles at all, called <strong>default</strong>.</p>
<p>For a simple website, a single &apos;Section&apos; (called &apos;articles&apos;, for example) may be all that&apos;s required.</p>

'warn_mail_unavailable'
'Diagnostics: Your PHP installation is missing the mail() function'
'PHP's "mail":https://php.net/manual/en/function.mail.php function is disabled. Therefore, Textpattern cannot send emails and some functionality will not be available. For example, email notifications for new comments will not be sent, and new users will not receive a welcome email. Users can still be added, however.'
<p><span class="caps">PHP</span>&apos;s <a href="https://php.net/manual/en/function.mail.php">mail</a> function is disabled. Therefore, Textpattern cannot send emails and some functionality will not be available. For example, email notifications for new comments will not be sent, and new users will not receive a welcome email. Users can still be added, however.</p>

'warn_register_globals_or_update'
'Diagnostics: Turn register_globals off or update to a newer PHP version'
'A severe vulnerability was discovered in certain versions of PHP concerning the controversial legacy feature "registered globals":https://php.net/manual/en/security.globals.php. "The security advisory":https://www.hardened-php.net/advisory_202005.79.html says: "can lead to unexpected behaviour of PHP applications, which can lead to execution of remote PHP code in many situations".

Vulnerable versions are PHP4 versions <= 4.4.0 and PHP5 versions <= 5.0.5. You should either update to a newer version or make sure that @register_globals@ is turned off. @register_globals@ was deprecated as of PHP 5.3.0 and removed as of PHP 5.4.0.

You can turn off @register_globals@ in the @php.ini@ file. If you are in a hosted environment, you may have an option in your hosting control panel for it (ask your host). When running PHP as an Apache module, it is also possible to turn @register_globals@ off via Apache's @.htaccess@ configuration files. Uncomment the following line from your @.htaccess@ file by removing the leading #:

bc. php_value register_globals 0'
<p>A severe vulnerability was discovered in certain versions of <span class="caps">PHP</span> concerning the controversial legacy feature <a href="https://php.net/manual/en/security.globals.php">registered globals</a>. <a href="https://www.hardened-php.net/advisory_202005.79.html">The security advisory</a> says: &quot;can lead to unexpected behaviour of <span class="caps">PHP</span> applications, which can lead to execution of remote <span class="caps">PHP</span> code in many situations&quot;.</p>
<p>Vulnerable versions are PHP4 versions &lt;= 4.4.0 and PHP5 versions &lt;= 5.0.5. You should either update to a newer version or make sure that <code>register_globals</code> is turned off. <code>register_globals</code> was deprecated as of <span class="caps">PHP</span> 5.3.0 and removed as of <span class="caps">PHP</span> 5.4.0.</p>
<p>You can turn off <code>register_globals</code> in the <code>php.ini</code> file. If you are in a hosted environment, you may have an option in your hosting control panel for it (ask your host). When running <span class="caps">PHP</span> as an Apache module, it is also possible to turn <code>register_globals</code> off via Apache&apos;s <code>.htaccess</code> configuration files. Uncomment the following line from your <code>.htaccess</code> file by removing the leading #:</p>
<pre><code>php_value register_globals 0
</code></pre>

		<li>can only modify article until is published</li>
		<li>can apply status of <em>only</em> Draft, Hidden or Pending</li>
	</ul></li>
		<li>can view other users&apos; articles</li>
		<li>can manage images and files</li>
		<li>can manage sections</li>
		<li>can manage pages, forms and style</li>
	</ul>

'blockquote'
'Block Quotes'
'To make an entire paragraph into a block quotation, place "bq. " before it.

Example

bq. Any old text.

bq. bq. A block quotation.

bq. Any old text.

Will become:

bq. <p>Any old text.</p>

bq. <blockquote>A block quotation.</blockquote>

bq. <p>Any old text.</p>

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

Any old text.

bq. A block quotation.

Any old text.

<small>NB: Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in &#60;p&#62; tags.</small>

"More information(blockquote reference at htmlhelp.com)":https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/block/blockquote.html'
<p>To make an entire paragraph into a block quotation, place &quot;bq. &quot; before it.</p>
<p>Example</p>

	<blockquote>Any old text.</blockquote>

	<blockquote>bq. A block quotation.</blockquote>

	<blockquote>Any old text.</blockquote>

<p>Will become:</p>

	<blockquote>Any old text.</blockquote>

	<blockquote>A block quotation.</blockquote>

	<blockquote>Any old text.</blockquote>

<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>
<p>Any old text.</p>

	<blockquote>A block quotation.</blockquote>

<p>Any old text.</p>
<p><small>NB: Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in &#60;p&#62; tags.</small></p>
<p><a href="https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/block/blockquote.html" title="blockquote reference at htmlhelp.com">More information</a></p>
 

'bold'
'Strong Emphasis'
'Use ==*asterisks*== for text to be *strongly emphasized*.

The &#60;strong&#62; <acronym title="Hypertext Markup Language">HTML</acronym> tag is applied:

bq. *a phrase* &rarr; &#60;strong&#62;a phrase&#60;/strong&#62; &rarr; *a phrase*

If the text is not meant to have *structural* emphasis, but simply to be styled in **bold** (e.g., as a design element) use double asterisks:

bq. ==**a phrase**== &rarr; &#60;b&#62;a phrase&#60;/b&#62; &rarr; **a phrase**

<small>NB: quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</small>

"More information(strong reference at htmlhelp.com)":https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/phrase/strong.html'
<p>Use *asterisks* for text to be <strong>strongly emphasized</strong>.</p>
<p>The &#60;strong&#62; <span class="caps">HTML</span> tag is applied:</p>

	<blockquote><strong>a phrase</strong> &rarr; &#60;strong&#62;a phrase&#60;/strong&#62; &rarr; <strong>a phrase</strong></blockquote>

<p>If the text is not meant to have <strong>structural</strong> emphasis, but simply to be styled in <b>bold</b> (e.g., as a design element) use double asterisks:</p>

	<blockquote>**a phrase** &rarr; &#60;b&#62;a phrase&#60;/b&#62; &rarr; <b>a phrase</b></blockquote>

<p><small>NB: quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</small></p>

	<li>A second item</li>
	<li>A third item</li>
	<li>A fourth item</li>

'css_mode'
'Preferences: CSS Mode'
'The "Cascading Style Sheets":https://www.w3schools.com/css/default.asp standard is a slim, powerful language that allows you to centralize site-wide control of content presentation. Everything from fonts and colours to sizes and positions can be governed through CSS.

Ordinarily CSS data appears in the <code>head</code> section of an HTML document, or is stored in a text file linked to the document. Modern browsers parse this data in advance of displaying a page, and then cache the data for use on subsequent pages or site visits. As such, CSS can greatly reduce the download size of web pages: just one of many improvements  CSS formatting offers over older web design methods.

Textpattern offers two modes of maintaining style sheets for your pages.

In Manual CSS mode, you can create (or paste in) any style sheet information you like, right in the Textpattern interface. This allows for quick editing of styles without the clumsy bother of ftp uploading. Style sheets can be generated from scratch or by WYSIWYG website editors, and are found "all over the web":https://directory.google.com/Top/Computers/Programming/Internet/CSS/Sample_Style_Sheets/Layouts/.

In Automatic CSS mode, style sheet editing is taken to a sophisticated new level, using an editing interface and organizational method intended to make CSS parameters more readable and logical. Any existing style sheet can be 'poured' into the editing interface and modified indefinitely.

In Automatic mode, CSS files are classified as either _layout_ or _style_. Pages on a site can share or combine either type.

Layout files define large structural parameters, such as the number and size of columns on a page, headers and footers, &c.

Style files in turn govern parameters like typography, colour, and the finer methods of positioning (e.g. padding and margins and relationships between objects on a page) not defined in layout files.'
<p>The <a href="https://www.w3schools.com/css/default.asp">Cascading Style Sheets</a> standard is a slim, powerful language that allows you to centralize site-wide control of content presentation. Everything from fonts and colours to sizes and positions can be governed through <span class="caps">CSS</span>.</p>
<p>Ordinarily <span class="caps">CSS</span> data appears in the <code>head</code> section of an <span class="caps">HTML</span> document, or is stored in a text file linked to the document. Modern browsers parse this data in advance of displaying a page, and then cache the data for use on subsequent pages or site visits. As such, <span class="caps">CSS</span> can greatly reduce the download size of web pages: just one of many improvements  <span class="caps">CSS</span> formatting offers over older web design methods.</p>
<p>Textpattern offers two modes of maintaining style sheets for your pages.</p>
<p>In Manual <span class="caps">CSS</span> mode, you can create (or paste in) any style sheet information you like, right in the Textpattern interface. This allows for quick editing of styles without the clumsy bother of ftp uploading. Style sheets can be generated from scratch or by <span class="caps">WYSIWYG</span> website editors, and are found <a href="https://directory.google.com/Top/Computers/Programming/Internet/CSS/Sample_Style_Sheets/Layouts/">all over the web</a>.</p>
<p>In Automatic <span class="caps">CSS</span> mode, style sheet editing is taken to a sophisticated new level, using an editing interface and organizational method intended to make <span class="caps">CSS</span> parameters more readable and logical. Any existing style sheet can be &apos;poured&apos; into the editing interface and modified indefinitely.</p>
<p>In Automatic mode, <span class="caps">CSS</span> files are classified as either <em>layout</em> or <em>style</em>. Pages on a site can share or combine either type.</p>
<p>Layout files define large structural parameters, such as the number and size of columns on a page, headers and footers, &c.</p>
<p>Style files in turn govern parameters like typography, colour, and the finer methods of positioning (e.g. padding and margins and relationships between objects on a page) not defined in layout files.</p>

<p>This option will affect how article dates shall appear on the front page of your site only.</p>

<small>NB: quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</small>

"More information(del reference at htmlhelp.com)":https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/phrase/del.html'

'file_max_upload_size'
'Maximum Upload Size'
'Here you can restrict the maximim size (in bytes) for file uploads. Note that this value is also limited by the configuration of PHP. If Textpattern detects that your PHP configuration only allows smaller downloads it will automatically correct this value downwards. If you want to allow larger file uploads via upload from the browser you have to talk with your host. (The relevant values in php.ini are post_max_size, upload_max_filesize and memory_limit.)

Note: Image Uploads are *not* restricted by this value. The maximum allowed size for images is always ~ 1MB (unless restricted by PHP as explained above).'
<p>Here you can restrict the maximim size (in bytes) for file uploads. Note that this value is also limited by the configuration of PHP. If Textpattern detects that your PHP configuration only allows smaller downloads it will automatically correct this value downwards. If you want to allow larger file uploads via upload from the browser you have to talk with your host. (The relevant values in php.ini are post_max_size, upload_max_filesize and memory_limit.)</p>
<p>Note: Image Uploads are <strong>not</strong> restricted by this value. The maximum allowed size for images is always ~ 1MB (unless restricted by PHP as explained above).</p>


 

'form_articles'
'Article Tags'
'These tags are replaced with their corresponding values every time an article is output. 

_Author_
The full name of the article's author.

_Body_
The body of the article. Note that the body of articles processed with Textile will already have <code><p></code> tags around each paragraph. Thus it is superfluous to use additional paragraph tags. 

_Excerpt_
Text, if any, that was entered in the article's 'Excerpt' field.

_Category 1_
_Category 2_
Categories, if any, assigned to the article.

_Comments invite_
Will be replaced by the invitation for comments, if any, assigned to the article, as well as the current count of comments received.

_Permanent link_
Text, article date or graphic that represents a permanent link to the article.

_Posted_
The time associated with the article, in the format specified in the 'date format' items on the Texpattern preferences page. If this tag is placed between 'permanent link' tags, then the article's date becomes its clickable permanent link.

_Title_
Guess.'

<p><em>Category 1</em><br />

'header'
'Headers'
'To make an entire paragraph into a Header, place "h<em>n</em>. " at its beginning, where _n_ is a number from 1&#8211;6.

Examples:

==h1. Header 1==

==h2. Header 2==

==h3. Header 3==

Will become:

&#60;h1&#62;Header 1&#60;/h1&#62;

&#60;h2&#62;Header 2&#60;/h2&#62;

&#60;h3&#62;Header 3&#60;/h3&#62;

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

h1. Header 1

h2. Header 2

h3. Header 3

<small>NB: Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in &#60;p&#62; tags.</small>

"More information(H1 reference at htmlhelp.com)":https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/block/h1.html'
<p>To make an entire paragraph into a Header, place &quot;h<em>n</em>. &quot; at its beginning, where <em>n</em> is a number from 1&#8211;6.</p>
<p>Examples:</p>
<p>h1. Header 1</p>
<p>h2. Header 2</p>
<p>h3. Header 3</p>
<p>Will become:</p>
<p>&#60;h1&#62;Header 1&#60;/h1&#62;</p>
<p>&#60;h2&#62;Header 2&#60;/h2&#62;</p>
<p>&#60;h3&#62;Header 3&#60;/h3&#62;</p>
<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>

	<h1>Header 1</h1>

	<h2>Header 2</h2>

	<h3>Header 3</h3>

<p><small>NB: Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in &#60;p&#62; tags.</small></p>

'htaccess_missing'
'.htaccess File Missing'
'You are using a Permanent Link mode which requires an .htaccess file to be placed in your site directory. The recommended directives to place in this .htaccess file are:

<pre>RewriteEngine On
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -f [OR]
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -d
RewriteRule ^(.+) - [PT,L]</pre>

<pre>RewriteRule ^(.*) index.php</pre>'
<p>You are using a Permanent Link mode which requires an .htaccess file to be placed in your site directory. The recommended directives to place in this .htaccess file are:</p>
<pre>RewriteEngine On
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -f [OR]
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -d
RewriteRule ^(.+) - [PT,L]</pre>

<pre>RewriteRule ^(.*) index.php</pre>


 

'Images'
'To insert an image, put the URL(Universal Resource Locator) for the image inside exclamation marks.

Optional: text that immediately follows the URL in (parentheses) will be used as the Alt text for the image. Images on the web should always have descriptive Alt text for the benefit of readers using non-graphical browsers.

Optional: place a colon followed by a URL immediately after the closing ! to make the image into a link.

Example:
 ==!https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif(Textist)!==

With a link:

 ==!/common/textist.gif(Textist)!:https://textism.com==

Will become:

==&#60;img src="https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif" alt="Textist" /&#62;==

With a link:

==&#60;a href="https://textism.com"&#62;&#60;img src="/common/textist.gif" alt="Textist" /&#62;&#60;/a&#62;==

Which the browser will use to render the image:

<img src="https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif" />

With a link:

<a href="https://www.textism.com"><img src="https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif" border="0" /></a>

<a href="https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/special/img.html" target="_blank" title="img tag reference at htmlhelp.com">More information</a>'
<p>To insert an image, put the <acronym title="Universal Resource Locator">URL</acronym> for the image inside exclamation marks.</p>
<p>Optional: text that immediately follows the <span class="caps">URL</span> in (parentheses) will be used as the Alt text for the image. Images on the web should always have descriptive Alt text for the benefit of readers using non-graphical browsers.</p>
<p>Optional: place a colon followed by a <span class="caps">URL</span> immediately after the closing ! to make the image into a link.</p>
<p>Example:</p>
 !https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif(Textist)!

<p>With a link:</p>

 !/common/textist.gif(Textist)!:https://textism.com

<p>Will become:</p>
<p>&#60;img src="https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif" alt="Textist" /&#62;</p>
<p>With a link:</p>
<p>&#60;a href="https://textism.com"&#62;&#60;img src="/common/textist.gif" alt="Textist" /&#62;&#60;/a&#62;</p>
<p>Which the browser will use to render the image:</p>
<p><img src="https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif" /></p>
<p>With a link:</p>

If you choose to import from a file - that is, MovableType or Blogger - you must place it under the /include/import directory of your textpattern install dir, and call it @import.txt@.

'intro'
'What Textile Does'
'* Replace single and double primes ==(&#39; and &#34;)== used as quotation marks with HTML(HyperText Markup Language) entities for opening and closing quotation marks (&apos;&apos; and &quot;&quot;) in readable text, while leaving untouched the primes required within HTML tags.

* Replace double hyphens ==(--)== with an em-dash (&#8212;) entity.

* Replace single hyphens surrounded by spaces with an en-dash (&#8211;) entity.

* Replace triplets of periods (...) with an ellipsis (&#8230;) entity.

* Convert many nonstandard characters (&#376;&#250;&#223;&#352;&#960;&#338;) to browser-safe entities corresponding to keyboard input.

* Apply block- and phrase-level structural tags automatically and at the discretion of the writer via quick tags.

* Allow the quick creation of simple and complex tables

* Allow the simple application of style, class, id, language and alignment attributes to elements

* Create hyperlinks and insert images via quick tags.

* Define acronyms via quick tags

* Wrap an &#60;acronym&#62; tag around runs of three or more capital letters automatically.

* Convert ==(TM)==, ==(R)==, and ==(C)== to &trade;, &reg;, and &copy;</li>

* Convert the letter x to a dimension sign: ==2x4== to 2&times;4 and 8 ==x== 10 to 8&times;10'
'<ul>
		<li>Replace single and double primes (&amp;#39; and &amp;#34;) used as quotation marks with <acronym title="HyperText Markup Language">HTML</acronym> entities for opening and closing quotation marks (&apos;&apos; and &quot;&quot;) in readable text, while leaving untouched the primes required within <span class="caps">HTML</span> tags.</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Replace double hyphens (--) with an em-dash (&#8212;) entity.</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Replace single hyphens surrounded by spaces with an en-dash (&#8211;) entity.</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Replace triplets of periods (...) with an ellipsis (&#8230;) entity.</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Convert many nonstandard characters (&#376;&#250;&#223;&#352;&#960;&#338;) to browser-safe entities corresponding to keyboard input.</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Apply block- and phrase-level structural tags automatically and at the discretion of the writer via quick tags.</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Allow the quick creation of simple and complex tables</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Allow the simple application of style, class, id, language and alignment attributes to elements</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Create hyperlinks and insert images via quick tags.</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Define acronyms via quick tags</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Wrap an &#60;acronym&#62; tag around runs of three or more capital letters automatically.</li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Convert (TM), (R), and (C) to &trade;, &reg;, and &copy;</li></li>
	</ul>

	<ul>
		<li>Convert the letter x to a dimension sign: 2x4 to 2&times;4 and 8 x 10 to 8&times;10</li>
	</ul>

'italic'

'Use ==_underscores_== around text to be _emphasized._

The &#60;em&#62; HTML(HyperText Markup Language) tag is applied:

==_a phrase_=='
<p>Use _underscores_ around text to be <em>emphasized.</em></p>
<p>The &#60;em&#62; <acronym title="HyperText Markup Language"><span class="caps">HTM</span>L</acronym> tag is applied:</p>
<p>_a phrase_&#160;&#160;&rarr;&#160;&#160;&#60;em&#62;a phrase&#60;/em&#62; &rarr; <em>a phrase</em></p>
<p>If the text is not meant to have structural emphasis, but simply to be styled in italic (e.g., a phrase in a foreign language) use double underscores:</p>
<p>__a phrase__ &rarr; &#60;i&#62;a phrase&#60;/i&#62; &rarr; <i>a phrase</i></p>
<p>NB: quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</p>

'Links'
'To make a hypertext link, put the link text in =="quotation marks"== followed immediately by a colon and the <acronym title="Universal Resource Locator">URL</acronym> of the link.

Optional: text in (parentheses) following the link text, but before the closing quotation mark, will become a Title attribute for the link, visible as a tool tip when a cursor is above it.

Example:

 =="This is a link (This is a title)== ==":https://www.textism.com== 

Will become:

&#60;a href=&#34;https://www.textism.com&#34; title=&#34;This is a title&#34;&#62;This is a link&#60;/a&#62;

Which, when displayed in a browser, will look like this:

<a href="https://www.textism.com" title="This is a title" style="color:blue">This is a link</a>

*New* in Textile 2.0 is the ability to store long ugly urls outside of the text you're working in, and reference them with aliases of your choosing.

Simply store the url anywhere in the document,  such as at the end, using this syntax:

[alias (optional title)]https://url

Each referenced url must begin on a new line.

Example text:
This is a &#34;link&#34;:bob to Bob's website.

So long as this appears elsewhere in the document:
[bob]https://itsbob.com/index.html

...the final output will be:
This is a &#60;a href=&#34;https://itsbob.com/index.html&#34;&#62;link&#60;/a&#62; to Bob's website.

<a href="https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/special/a.html" target="_blank" title="a tag reference at htmlhelp.com">More information</a>'
<p>To make a hypertext link, put the link text in "quotation marks" followed immediately by a colon and the <acronym title="Universal Resource Locator"><span class="caps">URL</span></acronym> of the link.</p>

<p>"This is a link (This is a title) ":https://www.textism.com</p>

'override_emailcharset'
'Override the default characterset for emails'
'Textpattern uses utf-8 wherever possible per default, also for outgoing emails. However some older email clients (like some versions of Outlook) do not have proper utf-8 support. If you see garbled characters in E-Mails, this setting will try to convert mails to ISO-8859-1 before sending them.

Note: If you are using a language the characters of which cannot be represented in ISO-8859-1, this setting will unfortunately not help you. You should try finding a utf-8 (or unicode)-compatible email-client.'
<p>Textpattern uses utf-8 wherever possible per default, also for outgoing emails. However some older email clients (like some versions of Outlook) do not have proper utf-8 support. <p>Note: If you are using a language the characters of which cannot be represented in ISO-8859-1, this setting will unfortunately not help you. You should try finding a utf-8 (or unicode)-compatible email-client.</p>

'In *Debugging* mode, all PHP error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins.  In addition, the following information will be visible when you view the XHTML source of live pages:

* the time it took the server to build the page ('Runtime')
* the number of MySQL queries executed
* approximate peak memory usage
* all Textpattern tags encountered while building the page

In *Testing* mode, PHP error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins. The following information will be visible when you view the XHTML source of live pages:

* the time it took the server to build the page ('Runtime')
* the number of MySQL queries executed
* approximate peak memory usage

In *Live* mode, no error reporting or diagnostic information will be sent to the page. Generally, Live mode is preferred, as it imposes the least overhead on the server.'
