'section_category'
'Sections'
'(An excellent "article":https://www.jdueck.net/articles/textpattern.html on the semantics of sections and categories in Textpattern has been written by Joel Dueck.)

Sections in Textpattern are perhaps best thought of as analogous to sections in a newspaper: each belong to the same publication, but each might feature a different layout, or a different style of article, and so on. 

For most websites, only a bare minimum of sections need be created.

A likely scenario for a personal site, with three sections:

*archive*: the section to which all articles that appear on the front page belong. Whenever a reader clicks on an article's permanent link (for example https://thissite/archive/123/), it will be displayed in the page layout assigned to this section. To go to a paged list of the most recent articles assigned to the section _archive_, the url would be https://thissite/archive/

*about*: an 'about this site' page, which differs from the front page and the _archive_ section, in that it is more static, may have a different layout, and will likely contain only one article. The about section would be reached at https://thissite/about/.

*photos*: A section whose differing layout (and possibly different CSS) would display photos instead of articles.

Note that a special hidden 'section' always exists, named *default*. Using the layout called 'default' as well as the CSS called 'default', this is what makes up the front page of your site. '
<p>(An excellent <a href="https://www.jdueck.net/articles/textpattern.html">article</a> on the semantics of sections and categories in Textpattern has been written by Joel Dueck.)</p>
<p>Sections in Textpattern are perhaps best thought of as analogous to sections in a newspaper: each belong to the same publication, but each might feature a different layout, or a different style of article, and so on. </p>
<p>For most websites, only a bare minimum of sections need be created.</p>
<p>A likely scenario for a personal site, with three sections:</p>
<p><strong>archive</strong>: the section to which all articles that appear on the front page belong. Whenever a reader clicks on an article&apos;s permanent link (for example https://thissite/archive/123/), it will be displayed in the page layout assigned to this section. To go to a paged list of the most recent articles assigned to the section <em>archive</em>, the url would be https://thissite/archive/</p>
<p><strong>about</strong>: an &apos;about this site&apos; page, which differs from the front page and the <em>archive</em> section, in that it is more static, may have a different layout, and will likely contain only one article. The about section would be reached at https://thissite/about/.</p>
<p><strong>photos</strong>: A section whose differing layout (and possibly different <span class="caps">CSS</span>) would display photos instead of articles.</p>
<p>Note that a special hidden &apos;section&apos; always exists, named <strong>default</strong>. Using the layout called &apos;default&apos; as well as the <span class="caps">CSS</span> called &apos;default&apos;, this is what makes up the front page of your site. </p>

If most of your articles end up belonging to this section, then set this to 'yes'.

Note that only one section at a time can be set to be selected by default.'

<p>If most of your articles end up belonging to this section, then set this to &apos;yes&apos;.</p>
<p>Note that only one section at a time can be set to be selected by default.</p>

'section_searchable'

'send_lastmod'
'Send Last-Modified Header'
'When set, Textpattern will read a visitor's HTTP(HyperText Transfer Protocol) @If-Modified-Since@ header (if one exists) and compares it to the last site update. If nothing has changed since the visitor last loaded the page (i.e., if the timestamp the browser sends is the same as the last site update), then a header is sent back instructing the visitor's browser to use its cached version of the page.

This can reduce bandwidth consumption, page load times, and reduce the load on the webserver.'
<p>When set, Textpattern will read a visitor&apos;s <acronym title="HyperText Transfer Protocol">HTTP</acronym> <code>If-Modified-Since</code> header (if one exists) and compares it to the last site update. If nothing has changed since the visitor last loaded the page (i.e., if the timestamp the browser sends is the same as the last site update), then a header is sent back instructing the visitor&apos;s browser to use its cached version of the page.</p>
<p>This can reduce bandwidth consumption, page load times, and reduce the load on the webserver.</p>

@/images/12t.jpg@

'url_mode'
'URL Mode'
'Governs how permanent links to archived articles will appear. 

?=messy mode is the most straightforward; it will work without you having to do much mucking about. In this mode URLs for archived articles on your site will look like this:

@https://www.yoursite.com/index.php?id=123@

/clean/ mode is a little trickier, but it makes URLs that are elegant and easier to remember. Here are the steps to make /clean/ mode work:

You must have 'Use sections' set in Site Preferences

Your site must be on a Unix-based server running Apache, or on a Windows server running  PHP as an Apache module

For every section you have on your site (including the default section called 'article'), place a text file with the exact same name in your root web directory, containing the following bit of code, replacing SECTION_NAME with the section's actual name:

<code>
<?php
	include "./textpattern/config.php"; 
	$s = "SECTION_NAME";
	include $txpcfg["txpath"]."/publish.php";
	textpattern();
?>
</code>

Next, with a text editor create or open an existing file in your web root called <code>.htaccess</code> and paste the following inside:

<code>
<Files SECTION_NAME> 
ForceType application/x-httpd-php 
</Files> 
</code>

again, replace SECTION_NAME with the section's actual name.

Once you select /clean/ url mode in Site Preferences, your permanent link urls will look more familiar:

@https://www.mysite.com/article/123/@'
<p>Governs how permanent links to archived articles will appear. </p>
<p>?=messy mode is the most straightforward; it will work without you having to do much mucking about. In this mode URLs for archived articles on your site will look like this:</p>

<code>https://www.yoursite.com/index.php?id=123</code>

<p>/clean/ mode is a little trickier, but it makes URLs that are elegant and easier to remember. Here are the steps to make /clean/ mode work:</p>
<p>You must have &apos;Use sections&apos; set in Site Preferences</p>
<p>Your site must be on a Unix-based server running Apache, or on a Windows server running  <span class="caps">PHP</span> as an Apache module</p>
<p>For every section you have on your site (including the default section called &apos;article&apos;), place a text file with the exact same name in your root web directory, containing the following bit of code, replacing <span class="caps">SECTION</span>_NAME with the section&apos;s actual name:</p>

<code><br />
&lt;?php<br />
include "./textpattern/config.php"; <br />
$s = "SECTION_NAME";<br />
include $txpcfg["txpath"]."/publish.php";<br />
textpattern();<br />
?&gt;<br />
</code>

<p>Next, with a text editor create or open an existing file in your web root called <code>.htaccess</code> and paste the following inside:</p>

<code><br />
&lt;Files SECTION_NAME&gt; <br />
ForceType application/x-httpd-php <br />
&lt;/Files&gt; <br />
</code>

<p>again, replace <span class="caps">SECTION</span>_NAME with the section&apos;s actual name.</p>
<p>Once you select /clean/ url mode in Site Preferences, your permanent link urls will look more familiar:</p>

<code>https://www.mysite.com/article/123/</code>

'Sections in Textpattern are significant in that they don't merely imply a type to which articles belong. Each section has attached to it a layout (either created by you or generated by Textpattern), a style in the form of a CSS(Cascading Style Sheet) file, and a physical location on the webserver.

When posting new articles, all this information, which is pertinent to the future display of the article, is attached through the choice of Section.

That said, the first Section we should address is one that can't be attached to articles at all, called *default*. This collection of 

For a simple website, a single section (called 'articles', for example) may be all that's required. '

<p>When posting new articles, all this information, which is pertinent to the future display of the article, is attached through the choice of Section.</p>

'warn_mail_unavailable'
'Diagnostics: Your PHP installation is missing the mail() function'
'On your host PHP is not compiled with a mail() function. Therefore Textpattern cannot send emails, and you will not be able to use some functions. For example you won't be able to receive email-notifications on comments, and you won't be able to add other users (well, you can add them, but they won't receive the email with their password).'
<p>On your host <span class="caps">PHP</span> is not compiled with a mail() function. Therefore Textpattern cannot send emails, and you will not be able to use some functions. For example you won&apos;t be able to receive email-notifications on comments, and you won&apos;t be able to add other users (well, you can add them, but they won&apos;t receive the email with their password).</p>

'warn_register_globals_or_update'
'Turn register_globals off or update to a newer PHP version.'
'Recently a severe vulnerability was discovered in PHP. "The security advisory ":https://www.hardened-php.net/advisory_202005.79.html says:

bq. can lead to unexpected behaviour of PHP applications, which can lead to execution of remote PHP code in many situations.

Vulnerable versions are PHP4-versions <= 4.4.0 and PHP5-versions <= 5.0.5. You should either update to newer versions or make sure that register_globals is turned off.

You can turn off register_globals in the php.ini file. If you are in a hosted environment, you may have an option in your hosting control panel for it (ask your host). When using mod_php (php as an apache module), it is also possible to turn register_globals off via .htaccess. Uncomment the following line from your .htaccess:

@php_value register_globals off@
'
<p>Recently a severe vulnerability was discovered in PHP. <a href="https://www.hardened-php.net/advisory_202005.79.html">The security advisory</a> says:</p>

	<blockquote>
	<p>can lead to unexpected behaviour of PHP applications, which can lead to execution of remote PHP code in many situations.</p>
	</blockquote>

<p>Vulnerable versions are PHP4-versions <= 4.4.0 and PHP5-versions <= 5.0.5. You should either update to newer versions or make sure that register_globals is turned off.</p>
<p>You can turn off register_globals in the php.ini file. If you are in a hosted environment, you may have an option in your hosting control panel for it (ask your host). When using mod_php (php as an apache module), it is also possible to turn register_globals off via .htaccess. Uncomment the following line from your .htaccess:</p>

<code>php_value register_globals off</code>



 

'plugin_cache_dir'
'Preferences: Plugin cache directory path'
'This setting is mainly used for plugin developers. When specified, you can put the plugin source file (not compiled) in this directory and Textpattern will load it automatically, so you can easily edit the plugin and immediately see the effect it has without having to install/activate the plugin. *Beware* though that by using this method, a broken plugin (which fails to compile) may also break Textpattern!

It is recommended that you load plugins through the standard 'Plugins' administration panel on a production site, rather than using the plugin cache directory.

p(alert-block information). *Note:* Do not specify the same directory for both 'temporary' and 'plugin cache', as this will cause problems (Textpattern would try to run the contents of temporary log files as plugins).'
<p>This setting is mainly used for plugin developers. When specified, you can put the plugin source file (not compiled) in this directory and Textpattern will load it automatically, so you can easily edit the plugin and immediately see the effect it has without having to install/activate the plugin. <strong>Beware</strong> though that by using this method, a broken plugin (which fails to compile) may also break Textpattern!</p>
<p>It is recommended that you load plugins through the standard &apos;Plugins&apos; administration panel on a production site, rather than using the plugin cache directory.</p>
<p class="alert-block information"><strong>Note:</strong> Do not specify the same directory for both &apos;temporary&apos; and &apos;plugin cache&apos;, as this will cause problems (Textpattern would try to run the contents of temporary log files as plugins).</p>

'about_privileges'
'th-th'

'comments_auto_append'
'Preferences: Automatically append comments to articles?'
'This setting determines whether comments and the comment form are automatically appended to all individual articles (by automatically including the contents of the <a href="https://www.textpattern.net/wiki/index.php?title=Default_Forms" target="_blank">@comments_display@ form</a>).

The majority of the time, you will want this set to 'No', as this allows greater flexibility to the positioning of your comments in your site design. You will need to add the <a href="https://docs.textpattern.com/tags/comments" target="_blank">@<txp:comments />@ tag</a> manually in any article-based templates where you want comments to appear.

If you are experiencing duplicate display of comments in an article, then chances are your templates have the @<txp:comments />@ tag in a template but also have 'Automatically append comments to articles?' set to 'Yes' too - see "the related FAQ entry":https://textpattern.com/faq/95/comment-display-confusion for more information.'
<p>This setting determines whether comments and the comment form are automatically appended to all individual articles (by automatically including the contents of the <a href="https://www.textpattern.net/wiki/index.php?title=Default_Forms" target="_blank"><code>comments_display</code> form</a>).</p>
<p>The majority of the time, you will want this set to &apos;No&apos;, as this allows greater flexibility to the positioning of your comments in your site design. You will need to add the <a href="https://docs.textpattern.com/tags/comments" target="_blank"><code>&lt;txp:comments /&gt;</code> tag</a> manually in any article-based templates where you want comments to appear.</p>
<p>If you are experiencing duplicate display of comments in an article, then chances are your templates have the <code>&lt;txp:comments /&gt;</code> tag in a template but also have &apos;Automatically append comments to articles?&apos; set to &apos;Yes&apos; too &#8211; see <a href="https://textpattern.com/faq/95/comment-display-confusion">the related <span class="caps">FAQ</span> entry</a> for more information.</p>

'warn_register_globals_or_update'
'Turn register_globals off or update to a newer PHP version.'
'Recently a severe vulnerability was discovered in PHP. "The security advisory ":https://www.hardened-php.net/advisory_202005.79.html says:

bq. can lead to unexpected behaviour of PHP applications, which can lead to execution of remote PHP code in many situations.

Vulnerable versions are PHP4-versions <= 4.4.0 and PHP5-versions <= 5.0.5. You should either update to newer versions or make sure that register_globals is turned off.

You can turn off register_globals in the php.ini file. If you are in a hosted environment, you may have an option in your hosting control panel for it (ask your host). When using mod_php (php as an apache module), it is also possible to turn register_globals off via .htaccess. Uncomment the following line from your .htaccess:

@php_value register_globals off@
'
<p>Recently a severe vulnerability was discovered in <span class="caps">PHP</span>. <a href="https://www.hardened-php.net/advisory_202005.79.html">The security advisory</a> says:</p>

	<blockquote>
	<p>can lead to unexpected behaviour of <span class="caps">PHP</span> applications, which can lead to execution of remote <span class="caps">PHP</span> code in many situations.</p>
	</blockquote>

<p>Vulnerable versions are PHP4-versions &#60;= 4.4.0 and PHP5-versions &#60;= 5.0.5. You should either update to newer versions or make sure that register_globals is turned off.</p>
<p>You can turn off register_globals in the php.ini file. If you are in a hosted environment, you may have an option in your hosting control panel for it (ask your host). When using mod_php (php as an apache module), it is also possible to turn register_globals off via .htaccess. Uncomment the following line from your .htaccess:</p>
<p><code>php_value register_globals off</code></p>

'warn_mail_unavailable'
'Diagnostics: Your PHP installation is missing the mail() function'
'On your host PHP is not compiled with a mail() function. Therefore Textpattern cannot send emails, and you will not be able to use some functions. For example you won't be able to receive email-notifications on comments, and you won't be able to add other users (well, you can add them, but they won't receive the email with their password).'
<p>On your host <span class="caps">PHP</span> is not compiled with a mail() function. Therefore Textpattern cannot send emails, and you will not be able to use some functions. For example you won&apos;t be able to receive email-notifications on comments, and you won&apos;t be able to add other users (well, you can add them, but they won&apos;t receive the email with their password).</p>

'using_sections'
'manual'
'Using Sections'
'Sections in Textpattern are significant in that they don't merely imply a type to which articles belong. Each section has attached to it a layout (either created by you or generated by Textpattern), a style in the form of a CSS(Cascading Style Sheet) file, and a physical location on the webserver.

When posting new articles, all this information, which is pertinent to the future display of the article, is attached through the choice of Section.

That said, the first Section we should address is one that can't be attached to articles at all, called *default*. This collection of 

For a simple website, a single section (called 'articles', for example) may be all that's required. '
<p>Sections in Textpattern are significant in that they don&apos;t merely imply a type to which articles belong. Each section has attached to it a layout (either created by you or generated by Textpattern), a style in the form of a <acronym title="Cascading Style Sheet"><span class="caps">CSS</span></acronym> file, and a physical location on the webserver.</p>
<p>When posting new articles, all this information, which is pertinent to the future display of the article, is attached through the choice of Section.</p>
<p>That said, the first Section we should address is one that can&apos;t be attached to articles at all, called <strong>default</strong>. This collection of </p>
<p>For a simple website, a single section (called &apos;articles&apos;, for example) may be all that&apos;s required. </p>

'use_textile'
'Preferences: Use Textile'
'Textile is a utility intended to simplify the conversion of plain text to HTML. 

When enabled, Textile will process the body text of articles and return a structurally sound XHTML version. 

Textile also offers a powerful but easy-to-learn set of commands with which to format text,  define structures such as lists, headers and blockquotes, and insert links and images.'
<p>Textile is a utility intended to simplify the conversion of plain text to <span class="caps">HTML</span>. </p>

'use_mail_on_feeds_id'
'Preferences: Use mail on feeds id'
'Feed items in Atom, require a unique ID. To generate IDs unique to your site, Textpattern allows you to choose between either an email adress or your domain name to include in these IDs. The email-adress will be the first one used when creating the site. Unless you are likely going to be changing your domain-name, it is probably safe to set this to no.'
<p>Feed items in Atom, require a unique ID. To generate IDs unique to your site, Textpattern allows you to choose between either an email adress or your domain name to include in these IDs. The email-adress will be the first one used when creating the site. Unless you are likely going to be changing your domain-name, it is probably safe to set this to no.</p>

'url_title'
'URL Title'
'If you would like to have a specific title attached to the permalink url for your article, enter it here. If it is left blank, the URL title will be generated from the actual article title. 

This is particularly useful if the title of your article uses non-ascii characters.'
<p>If you would like to have a specific title attached to the permalink url for your article, enter it here. If it is left blank, the <span class="caps">URL</span> title will be generated from the actual article title. </p>
<p>This is particularly useful if the title of your article uses non-ascii characters.</p>

'url_mode'
'URL Mode'
'Governs how permanent links to archived articles will appear. 

?=messy mode is the most straightforward; it will work without you having to do much mucking about. In this mode URLs for archived articles on your site will look like this:

@https://www.yoursite.com/index.php?id=123@

/clean/ mode is a little trickier, but it makes URLs that are elegant and easier to remember. Here are the steps to make /clean/ mode work:

You must have 'Use sections' set in Site Preferences

Your site must be on a Unix-based server running Apache, or on a Windows server running  PHP as an Apache module

For every section you have on your site (including the default section called 'article'), place a text file with the exact same name in your root web directory, containing the following bit of code, replacing SECTION_NAME with the section's actual name:

<code>
<?php
	include "./textpattern/config.php"; 
	$s = "SECTION_NAME";
	include $txpcfg["txpath"]."/publish.php";
	textpattern();
?>
</code>

Next, with a text editor create or open an existing file in your web root called <code>.htaccess</code> and paste the following inside:

<code>
<Files SECTION_NAME> 
ForceType application/x-httpd-php 
</Files> 
</code>

again, replace SECTION_NAME with the section's actual name.

Once you select /clean/ url mode in Site Preferences, your permanent link urls will look more familiar:

@https://www.mysite.com/article/123/@'
<p>Governs how permanent links to archived articles will appear. </p>
<p>?=messy mode is the most straightforward; it will work without you having to do much mucking about. In this mode <span class="caps">URL</span>s for archived articles on your site will look like this:</p>
<p><code>https://www.yoursite.com/index.php?id=123</code></p>
<p>/clean/ mode is a little trickier, but it makes <span class="caps">URL</span>s that are elegant and easier to remember. Here are the steps to make /clean/ mode work:</p>
<p>You must have &apos;Use sections&apos; set in Site Preferences</p>
<p>Your site must be on a Unix-based server running Apache, or on a Windows server running  <span class="caps">PHP</span> as an Apache module</p>
<p>For every section you have on your site (including the default section called &apos;article&apos;), place a text file with the exact same name in your root web directory, containing the following bit of code, replacing <span class="caps">SECTION</span>_NAME with the section&apos;s actual name:</p>
<p><code>
&#60;?php
	include &#34;./textpattern/config.php&#34;; 
	$s = &#34;SECTION_NAME&#34;;
	include $txpcfg[&#34;txpath&#34;].&#34;/publish.php&#34;;
	textpattern();
?&#62;
</code></p>
<p>Next, with a text editor create or open an existing file in your web root called <code>.htaccess</code> and paste the following inside:</p>
<p><code>
&#60;Files SECTION_NAME&#62; 
ForceType application/x-httpd-php 
&#60;/Files&#62; 
</code></p>
<p>again, replace <span class="caps">SECTION</span>_NAME with the section&apos;s actual name.</p>
<p>Once you select /clean/ url mode in Site Preferences, your permanent link urls will look more familiar:</p>
<p><code>https://www.mysite.com/article/123/</code></p>

'upload_thumbnail'
'Upload Thumbnail'
'Full-size images can be assigned their own thumbnails. These are useful for linking to and indexing full-size images.

A reference to the thumbnail is exactly the same as the reference to the full-size image, with the addition of 't' to the filename.

For example, to reference a thumbnail associated with a fullsize image stored at 

@/images/12.jpg@

...you would use

@/images/12t.jpg@
'
<p>Full-size images can be assigned their own thumbnails. These are useful for linking to and indexing full-size images.</p>
<p>A reference to the thumbnail is exactly the same as the reference to the full-size image, with the addition of &apos;t&apos; to the filename.</p>
<p>For example, to reference a thumbnail associated with a fullsize image stored at </p>
<p><code>/images/12.jpg</code></p>
<p>&#8230;you would use</p>
<p><code>/images/12t.jpg</code></p>

'trackback'
'Trackback'
'Many content management systems offer trackbacks to individual articles or postings. 

At their simplest, trackbacks offer a way to say, 'I've said something about this article; here's the name of my site, a link to the mention, and a brief excerpt'. The other site's owner can then use the trackback information however she wishes.

Some have likened trackbacks to 'referrers you insert manually', but that's a little reductive.'
<p>Many content management systems offer trackbacks to individual articles or postings. </p>
<p>At their simplest, trackbacks offer a way to say, &apos;I&apos;ve said something about this article; here&apos;s the name of my site, a link to the mention, and a brief excerpt&apos;. The other site&apos;s owner can then use the trackback information however she wishes.</p>
<p>Some have likened trackbacks to &apos;referrers you insert manually&apos;, but that&apos;s a little reductive.</p>

'timestamp'
'Time Stamp'
'The time attributions of an article can be set for the moment it is published or revised, or to any other time.

The format of the date input is:

Year Month Day Hour(24hr) : Minute

Articles with timestamps greater than the current time (in the future) will not appear on the site until that time passes.'
<p>The time attributions of an article can be set for the moment it is published or revised, or to any other time.</p>
<p>The format of the date input is:</p>
<p>Year Month Day Hour(24hr) : Minute</p>
<p>Articles with timestamps greater than the current time (in the future) will not appear on the site until that time passes.</p>

==+==<ins>inserted text</ins>==+==

-<del>deleted text</del>-<br />

'tempdir'
'Temp folder'
'The Temp folder sets an _absolute path_ to a temporary directory. PHP needs to have write access to this directory (usually chmod 777). This folder is used for uploads of any kind (plugins, files or images).'
<p>The Temp folder sets an <em>absolute path</em> to a temporary directory. <span class="caps">PHP</span> needs to have write access to this directory (usually chmod 777). This folder is used for uploads of any kind (plugins, files or images).</p>

<p>If this is set to <em>no</em>, then feeds will always contain the full article bodies. If this is set to <em>yes</em>, feed items will contain an excerpt instead of the article-body where it is available.</p>

==^a phrase^== → <sup>a phrase</sup> → ^a phrase^

<small>NB: quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</small>

'spam_blacklists'
'Spam blacklists'
'The blacklist providers entered here, will be contacted when a comment is posted to your blog and the IP of the comment poster will be checked. If the IP is listed, it means there have been spamming complaints made about this IP in the past and a comment will be rejected.

You can enter a list of domain names that should be seperated by commas. Potential blacklists can be found at "dmoz":https://dmoz.org/Computers/Internet/Abuse/Spam/Blacklists/, but be aware that it is *your* responsibility to make sure you can trust those blacklist-providers before you add any.'
<p>The blacklist providers entered here, will be contacted when a comment is posted to your blog and the IP of the comment poster will be checked. If the IP is listed, it means there have been spamming complaints made about this IP in the past and a comment will be rejected.</p>

Potential blacklists can be found at <a href="https://dmoz.org/Computers/Internet/Abuse/Spam/Blacklists/">dmoz</a>, but be aware that it is <strong>your</strong> responsibility to make sure you can trust those blacklist-providers before you add any.</p>

'send_lastmod'
'Send Last-Modified Header'
'When set, Textpattern will read a visitor's HTTP(HyperText Transfer Protocol) @If-Modified-Since@ header (if one exists) and compares it to the last site update. If nothing has changed since the visitor last loaded the page (i.e., if the timestamp the browser sends is the same as the last site update), then a header is sent back instructing the visitor's browser to use its cached version of the page.

This can reduce bandwidth consumption, page load times, and reduce the load on the webserver.'
<p>When set, Textpattern will read a visitor&apos;s <acronym title="HyperText Transfer Protocol"><span class="caps">HTTP</span></acronym> <code>If-Modified-Since</code> header (if one exists) and compares it to the last site update. If nothing has changed since the visitor last loaded the page (i.e., if the timestamp the browser sends is the same as the last site update), then a header is sent back instructing the visitor&apos;s browser to use its cached version of the page.</p>
<p>This can reduce bandwidth consumption, page load times, and reduce the load on the webserver.</p>

'section_category'
'Sections'
'(An excellent "article":https://www.jdueck.net/articles/textpattern.html on the semantics of sections and categories in Textpattern has been written by Joel Dueck.)

Sections in Textpattern are perhaps best thought of as analogous to sections in a newspaper: each belong to the same publication, but each might feature a different layout, or a different style of article, and so on. 

For most websites, only a bare minimum of sections need be created.

A likely scenario for a personal site, with three sections:

*archive*: the section to which all articles that appear on the front page belong. Whenever a reader clicks on an article's permanent link (for example https://thissite/archive/123/), it will be displayed in the page layout assigned to this section. To go to a paged list of the most recent articles assigned to the section _archive_, the url would be https://thissite/archive/

*about*: an 'about this site' page, which differs from the front page and the _archive_ section, in that it is more static, may have a different layout, and will likely contain only one article. The about section would be reached at https://thissite/about/.

*photos*: A section whose differing layout (and possibly different CSS) would display photos instead of articles.

Note that a special hidden 'section' always exists, named *default*. Using the layout called 'default' as well as the CSS called 'default', this is what makes up the front page of your site. '
<p>(An excellent <a href="https://www.jdueck.net/articles/textpattern.html">article</a> on the semantics of sections and categories in Textpattern has been written by Joel Dueck.)</p>
<p>Sections in Textpattern are perhaps best thought of as analogous to sections in a newspaper: each belong to the same publication, but each might feature a different layout, or a different style of article, and so on. </p>
<p>For most websites, only a bare minimum of sections need be created.</p>
<p>A likely scenario for a personal site, with three sections:</p>
<p><strong>archive</strong>: the section to which all articles that appear on the front page belong. Whenever a reader clicks on an article&apos;s permanent link (for example https://thissite/archive/123/), it will be displayed in the page layout assigned to this section. To go to a paged list of the most recent articles assigned to the section <em>archive</em>, the url would be https://thissite/archive/</p>
<p><strong>about</strong>: an &apos;about this site&apos; page, which differs from the front page and the <em>archive</em> section, in that it is more static, may have a different layout, and will likely contain only one article. The about section would be reached at https://thissite/about/.</p>
<p><strong>photos</strong>: A section whose differing layout (and possibly different <span class="caps">CSS</span>) would display photos instead of articles.</p>
<p>Note that a special hidden &apos;section&apos; always exists, named <strong>default</strong>. Using the layout called &apos;default&apos; as well as the <span class="caps">CSS</span> called &apos;default&apos;, this is what makes up the front page of your site. </p>

'publish_expired_articles'
'Preferences: Publish Expired Articles'
'When set to "Yes", expired articles will continue to show on your site after their expiry date has elapsed. You can use @<txp:if_expired />@ to show alternate content or markup for expired articles.

When set to "No", expired articles will be removed from the public site and return a "410 Gone" HTTP status instead.  Visiors hitting an expired article will be shown a custom page template named "error_410" if it's present .'
<p>When set to &quot;Yes&quot;, expired articles will continue to show on your site after their expiry date has elapsed. You can use <code>&#60;txp:if_expired /&#62;</code> to show alternate content or markup for expired articles.</p>
<p>When set to &quot;No&quot;, expired articles will be removed from the public site and return a &quot;410 Gone&quot; <span class="caps">HTTP</span> status instead.  Visiors hitting an expired article will be shown a custom page template named &quot;error_410&quot; if it&apos;s present .</p>

'production_status'
'Production Status'
'In *Debugging* mode, all PHP error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins.  In addition, the following information will be visible when you view the XHTML source of live pages:

* the time it took the server to build the page ('Runtime')
* the number of MySQL queries executed
* approximate peak memory usage
* all Textpattern tags encountered while building the page

In *Testing* mode, PHP error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins. The following information will be visible when you view the XHTML source of live pages:

* the time it took the server to build the page ('Runtime')
* the number of MySQL queries executed
* approximate peak memory usage

In *Live* mode, no error reporting or diagnostic information will be sent to the page. Generally, Live mode is preferred, as it imposes the least overhead on the server.'
<p>In <strong>Debugging</strong> mode, all <span class="caps">PHP</span> error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins.  In addition, the following information will be visible when you view the <span class="caps">XHTML</span> source of live pages:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>the time it took the server to build the page (&apos;Runtime&apos;)</li>
		<li>the number of MySQL queries executed</li>
		<li>approximate peak memory usage</li>
		<li>all Textpattern tags encountered while building the page</li>
	</ul>

<p>In <strong>Testing</strong> mode, <span class="caps">PHP</span> error reporting on your live site will be visible, as will warnings about malfunctioning code and plugins. The following information will be visible when you view the <span class="caps">XHTML</span> source of live pages:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>the time it took the server to build the page (&apos;Runtime&apos;)</li>
		<li>the number of MySQL queries executed</li>
		<li>approximate peak memory usage</li>
	</ul>

<p>In <strong>Live</strong> mode, no error reporting or diagnostic information will be sent to the page. Generally, Live mode is preferred, as it imposes the least overhead on the server.</p>

'override_emailcharset'
'Override the default characterset for emails'
'Textpattern uses utf-8 wherever possible per default, also for outgoing emails. However some older email clients (like some versions of Outlook) do not have proper utf-8 support. If you see garbled characters in E-Mails, this setting will try to convert mails to ISO-8859-1 before sending them.

Note: If you are using a language the characters of which cannot be represented in ISO-8859-1, this setting will unfortunately not help you. You should try finding a utf-8 (or unicode)-compatible email-client.'
<p>Textpattern uses utf-8 wherever possible per default, also for outgoing emails. However some older email clients (like some versions of Outlook) do not have proper utf-8 support. If you see garbled characters in E-Mails, this setting will try to convert mails to <span class="caps">ISO</span>-8859-1 before sending them.</p>
<p>Note: If you are using a language the characters of which cannot be represented in <span class="caps">ISO</span>-8859-1, this setting will unfortunately not help you. You should try finding a utf-8 (or unicode)-compatible email-client.</p>

'numeric'
'Numeric Lists'
'To make a numbered list, place each item in its own paragraph, preceded by “# ”.

Example

<pre>
# A first item
# A second item
# A third item
# A fourth item
</pre>

Will become:

==<ol>
<li>A first item</li>
<li>A second item</li>
<li>A third item</li>
<li>A fourth item</li>
</ol>==

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

<ol>
<li>A first item</li>
<li>A second item</li>
<li>A third item</li>
<li>A fourth item</li>
</ol>

<small>NB: Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in <p> tags.</small>

<a href="https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/lists/ol.html" title="Ordered list reference at htmlhelp.com" target="_blank">More information</a>'

'link_category'
'Link Categories'
'Link categories are optional, and are useful if you want to display different groupings of links, e.g., 'Friends', 'Romans', 'Countrymen'.

Selective link lists such as these are output with the Textpattern 'linklist' tag like so:

<code><txp:linklist category="Friends" /></code>'
<p>Link categories are optional, and are useful if you want to display different groupings of links, e.g., &apos;Friends&apos;, &apos;Romans&apos;, &apos;Countrymen&apos;.</p>

<p><code>&#60;txp:linklist category=&#34;Friends&#34; /&#62;</code></p>

'Links'
'To make a hypertext link, put the link text in =="quotation marks"== followed immediately by a colon and the <acronym title="Universal Resource Locator">URL</acronym> of the link.

Optional: text in (parentheses) following the link text, but before the closing quotation mark, will become a Title attribute for the link, visible as a tool tip when a cursor is above it.

Example:

 =="This is a link (This is a title)== ==":https://www.textism.com== 

Will become:

<a href="https://www.textism.com" title="This is a title">This is a link</a>

Which, when displayed in a browser, will look like this:

<a href="https://www.textism.com" title="This is a title" style="color:blue">This is a link</a>

*New* in Textile 2.0 is the ability to store long ugly urls outside of the text you're working in, and reference them with aliases of your choosing.

Simply store the url anywhere in the document,  such as at the end, using this syntax:

[alias (optional title)]https://url

Each referenced url must begin on a new line.

Example text:
This is a "link":bob to Bob's website.

So long as this appears elsewhere in the document:
[bob]https://itsbob.com/index.html

...the final output will be:
This is a <a href="https://itsbob.com/index.html">link</a> to Bob's website.

<a href="https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/special/a.html" target="_blank" title="a tag reference at htmlhelp.com">More information</a>'

'intro'
'What Textile Does'
'* Replace single and double primes (@'@ and @"@) used as quotation marks with HTML(HyperText Markup Language) entities for opening and closing quotation marks (@‘’@ and @“”@) in readable text, while leaving untouched the primes required within HTML tags.
* Replace double hyphens (==--==) with an em-dash (—) entity.
* Replace single hyphens surrounded by spaces with an en-dash (–) entity.
* Replace triplets of periods (==...==) with an ellipsis (…) entity.
* Convert many nonstandard characters (ŸúßŠπŒ) to browser-safe entities corresponding to keyboard input.
* Apply block- and phrase-level structural tags automatically and at the discretion of the writer via quick tags.
* Allow the quick creation of simple and complex tables.
* Allow the simple application of style, class, id, language and alignment attributes to elements.
* Create hyperlinks and insert images via quick tags.
* Define acronyms via quick tags.
* Wrap an <acronym> tag around runs of three or more capital letters automatically.
* Convert @(TM)@, @(R)@, and @(C)@ to ™, ®, and ©.
* Convert the letter x to a dimension sign: @2x4@ to 2×4 and @8x10@ to 8×10.'
'<ul>
		<li>Replace single and double primes (<code>&#39;</code> and <code>&#34;</code>) used as quotation marks with <acronym title="HyperText Markup Language"><span class="caps">HTML</span></acronym> entities for opening and closing quotation marks (<code>‘’</code> and <code>“”</code>) in readable text, while leaving untouched the primes required within <span class="caps">HTML</span> tags.</li>
		<li>Replace double hyphens (--) with an em-dash (—) entity.</li>
		<li>Replace single hyphens surrounded by spaces with an en-dash (–) entity.</li>
		<li>Replace triplets of periods (...) with an ellipsis (…) entity.</li>
		<li>Convert many nonstandard characters (ŸúßŠπŒ) to browser-safe entities corresponding to keyboard input.</li>
		<li>Apply block- and phrase-level structural tags automatically and at the discretion of the writer via quick tags.</li>
		<li>Allow the quick creation of simple and complex tables.</li>
		<li>Allow the simple application of style, class, id, language and alignment attributes to elements.</li>
		<li>Create hyperlinks and insert images via quick tags.</li>
		<li>Define acronyms via quick tags.</li>
		<li>Wrap an <acronym> tag around runs of three or more capital letters automatically.</li>
		<li>Convert <code>(TM)</code>, <code>(R)</code>, and <code>(C)</code> to ™, ®, and ©.</li>
		<li>Convert the letter x to a dimension sign: <code>2x4</code> to 2×4 and <code>8x10</code> to 8×10.</li>
	</ul>

'insert'

'Use ==+pluses+== around text that has been +inserted+. This is useful in marking changes from one version of a document to the next.

==+a phrase+== → <ins>a phrase</ins> → +a phrase+

<small>NB: quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</small>

"More information(del reference at htmlhelp.com)":https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/phrase/ins.html'

'Importing Into Textpattern'
'You can import your data into Textpattern from the following publishing tools:

* Movable Type, both using an "MT import file":https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_mt or "directly from a MySQL DataBase":https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_mtdb
* Blogger, creating an import file following the MT format spec. You can find an explanation about this on "importing from Blogger":https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_blogger
* "WordPress":https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_wp - including custom prefixes for your databases, if you're using them
* "b2":https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_b2. Not recently updated, but we can offer this import option for the same price

If you choose to import from a file - that is, MovableType or Blogger - you must place it under the /include/import directory of your textpattern install dir, and call it @import.txt@.

'
<p>You can import your data into Textpattern from the following publishing tools:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>Movable Type, both using an <a href="https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_mt">MT import file</a> or <a href="https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_mtdb">directly from a MySQL DataBase</a></li>
		<li>Blogger, creating an import file following the MT format spec. You can find an explanation about this on <a href="https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_blogger">importing from Blogger</a></li>
		<li><a href="https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_wp">WordPress</a> &#8211; including custom prefixes for your databases, if you&apos;re using them</li>
		<li><a href="https://textpattern.com/help/?item=import_b2">b2</a>. Not recently updated, but we can offer this import option for the same price</li>
	</ul>

<p>If you choose to import from a file &#8211; that is, MovableType or Blogger &#8211; you must place it under the /include/import directory of your textpattern install dir, and call it <code>import.txt</code>.</p>

'If you'd like to customise the name of your images directory, you can do so here. You must first create the directory in the root of your textpattern installation, you should also make sure that PHP is able to write to that directory (usually chmod 777).

Unlike other directory settings, this path is always a _relative_ path (to the directory of your root textpattern installation). The default is _images_.'

'Images'
'To insert an image, put the URL(Universal Resource Locator) for the image inside exclamation marks.

Optional: text that immediately follows the URL in (parentheses) will be used as the Alt text for the image. Images on the web should always have descriptive Alt text for the benefit of readers using non-graphical browsers.

Optional: place a colon followed by a URL immediately after the closing ! to make the image into a link.

Example:
 ==!https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif(Textist)!==

With a link:

 ==!/common/textist.gif(Textist)!:https://textism.com==

Will become:

==<img src="https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif" alt="Textist" />==

With a link:

==<a href="https://textism.com"><img src="/common/textist.gif" alt="Textist" /></a>==

Which the browser will use to render the image:

<img src="https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif" />

With a link:

<a href="https://www.textism.com"><img src="https://www.textism.com/common/textist.gif" border="0" /></a>

<a href="https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/special/img.html" target="_blank" title="img tag reference at htmlhelp.com">More information</a>'
<p>To insert an image, put the <acronym title="Universal Resource Locator"><span class="caps">URL</span></acronym> for the image inside exclamation marks.</p>
<p>Optional: text that immediately follows the <span class="caps">URL</span> in (parentheses) will be used as the Alt text for the image. Images on the web should always have descriptive Alt text for the benefit of readers using non-graphical browsers.</p>
<p>Optional: place a colon followed by a <span class="caps">URL</span> immediately after the closing ! to make the image into a link.</p>

'htaccess_missing'
'.htaccess File Missing'
'You are using a Permanent Link mode which requires an .htaccess file to be placed in your site directory. The recommended directives to place in this .htaccess file are:

<pre>RewriteEngine On
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -f [OR]
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -d
RewriteRule ^(.+) - [PT,L]</pre>

<pre>RewriteRule ^(.*) index.php</pre>'
<p>You are using a Permanent Link mode which requires an .htaccess file to be placed in your site directory. The recommended directives to place in this .htaccess file are:</p>
<pre>RewriteEngine On
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -f [OR]
RewriteCond %{REQUEST_FILENAME} -d
RewriteRule ^(.+) - [PT,L]</pre>

<pre>RewriteRule ^(.*) index.php</pre>

'header'
'Headers'
'To make an entire paragraph into a Header, place "h<em>n</em>. " at its beginning, where _n_ is a number from 1–6.

Examples:

==h1. Header 1==

==h2. Header 2==

==h3. Header 3==

Will become:

<h1>Header 1</h1>

<h2>Header 2</h2>

<h3>Header 3</h3>

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

h1. Header 1

h2. Header 2

h3. Header 3

<small>NB: Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in <p> tags.</small>

"More information(H1 reference at htmlhelp.com)":https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/block/h1.html'
<p>To make an entire paragraph into a Header, place &quot;h<em>n</em>. &quot; at its beginning, where <em>n</em> is a number from 1–6.</p>
<p>Examples:</p>
<p>h1. Header 1</p>
<p>h2. Header 2</p>
<p>h3. Header 3</p>
<p>Will become:</p>

<h1>Header 1</h1>

<h2>Header 2</h2>

<h3>Header 3</h3>

<p>Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:</p>

	<h1>Header 1</h1>

	<h2>Header 2</h2>

	<h3>Header 3</h3>

<p><small>NB: Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in <p> tags.</small></p>

'form_place_article'
'Article Tags'
'These tags are replaced with their corresponding values every time an article is output. 

_Author_
The full name of the article's author.

_Body_
The body of the article. Note that the body of articles processed with Textile will already have <code><p></code> tags around each paragraph. Thus it is superfluous to use additional paragraph tags. 

_Excerpt_
Text, if any, that was entered in the article's 'Excerpt' field.

_Category 1_
_Category 2_
Categories, if any, assigned to the article.

_Comments invite_
Will be replaced by the invitation for comments, if any, assigned to the article, as well as the current count of comments received.

_Permanent link_
Text, article date or graphic that represents a permanent link to the article.

_Posted_
The time associated with the article, in the format specified in the 'date format' items on the Texpattern preferences page. If this tag is placed between 'permanent link' tags, then the article's date becomes its clickable permanent link.

_Title_
Guess.'
<p>These tags are replaced with their corresponding values every time an article is output. </p>

<p><em>Excerpt</em><br />
Text, if any, that was entered in the article&apos;s &apos;Excerpt&apos; field.</p>

Categories, if any, assigned to the article.</p>

'file_max_upload_size'
'Maximum Upload Size'
'Here you can restrict the maximim size (in bytes) for file uploads. Note that this value is also limited by the configuration of PHP. If Textpattern detects that your PHP configuration only allows smaller downloads it will automatically correct this value downwards. If you want to allow larger file uploads via upload from the browser you have to talk with your host. (The relevant values in php.ini are post_max_size, upload_max_filesize and memory_limit.)

Note: Image Uploads are *not* restricted by this value. The maximum allowed size for images is always ~ 1MB (unless restricted by PHP as explained above).'
<p>Here you can restrict the maximim size (in bytes) for file uploads. Note that this value is also limited by the configuration of <span class="caps">PHP</span>. If Textpattern detects that your <span class="caps">PHP</span> configuration only allows smaller downloads it will automatically correct this value downwards. If you want to allow larger file uploads via upload from the browser you have to talk with your host. (The relevant values in php.ini are post_max_size, upload_max_filesize and memory_limit.)</p>
<p>Note: Image Uploads are <strong>not</strong> restricted by this value. The maximum allowed size for images is always ~ 1MB (unless restricted by <span class="caps">PHP</span> as explained above).</p>

INSERT INTO `txp_server_help` VALUES 'css_mode'
'Preferences: CSS Mode'
'The "Cascading Style Sheets":https://www.w3schools.com/css/default.asp standard is a slim, powerful language that allows you to centralize site-wide control of content presentation. Everything from fonts and colours to sizes and positions can be governed through CSS.

Ordinarily CSS data appears in the <code>head</code> section of an HTML document, or is stored in a text file linked to the document. Modern browsers parse this data in advance of displaying a page, and then cache the data for use on subsequent pages or site visits. As such, CSS can greatly reduce the download size of web pages: just one of many improvements  CSS formatting offers over older web design methods.

Textpattern offers two modes of maintaining style sheets for your pages.

In Manual CSS mode, you can create (or paste in) any style sheet information you like, right in the Textpattern interface. This allows for quick editing of styles without the clumsy bother of ftp uploading. Style sheets can be generated from scratch or by WYSIWYG website editors, and are found "all over the web":https://directory.google.com/Top/Computers/Programming/Internet/CSS/Sample_Style_Sheets/Layouts/.

In Automatic CSS mode, style sheet editing is taken to a sophisticated new level, using an editing interface and organizational method intended to make CSS parameters more readable and logical. Any existing style sheet can be 'poured' into the editing interface and modified indefinitely.

In Automatic mode, CSS files are classified as either _layout_ or _style_. Pages on a site can share or combine either type.

Layout files define large structural parameters, such as the number and size of columns on a page, headers and footers, &c.

Style files in turn govern parameters like typography, colour, and the finer methods of positioning (e.g. padding and margins and relationships between objects on a page) not defined in layout files.'
<p>The <a href="https://www.w3schools.com/css/default.asp">Cascading Style Sheets</a> standard is a slim, powerful language that allows you to centralize site-wide control of content presentation. Everything from fonts and colours to sizes and positions can be governed through <span class="caps">CSS</span>.</p>
<p>Ordinarily <span class="caps">CSS</span> data appears in the <code>head</code> section of an <span class="caps">HTML</span> document, or is stored in a text file linked to the document. Modern browsers parse this data in advance of displaying a page, and then cache the data for use on subsequent pages or site visits. As such, <span class="caps">CSS</span> can greatly reduce the download size of web pages: just one of many improvements  <span class="caps">CSS</span> formatting offers over older web design methods.</p>
<p>Textpattern offers two modes of maintaining style sheets for your pages.</p>
<p>In Manual <span class="caps">CSS</span> mode, you can create (or paste in) any style sheet information you like, right in the Textpattern interface. <p>Style files in turn govern parameters like typography, colour, and the finer methods of positioning (e.g. padding and margins and relationships between objects on a page) not defined in layout files.</p>

'Preferences: Use nofollow on comments?'
'This will add an additional attribute to the links made in comments. It is intended to be a measure that takes away the incentive for comment-spammers and was initiated by a range of search engines and software-writers. You can find out more about it by reading "Google's announcement":https://googleblog.blogspot.com/2005/01/preventing-comment-spam.html .

Note: This setting does not influence the functionality or ranking of your site. It will (or may) prevent or diminish the influence of your comments when some search-engines rank sites linked to from those comments.'

'To make a bulleted (point form) list, place each item in its own paragraph, preceded by "* ".

Example

<pre>
  * A first item
  * A second item
  * A third item
  * A fourth item
</pre>

Will become:

<ul>
<li>A first item</li>
<li>A second item</li>
<li>A third item</li>
<li>A fourth item</li>
</ul>

<ul>
	<li>A first item</li>
	<li>A second item</li>
	<li>A third item</li>
	<li>A fourth item</li>
</ul>

'bold'
'Strong Emphasis'
'Use ==*asterisks*== for text to be *strongly emphasized*.

The <strong> <acronym title="Hypertext Markup Language">HTML</acronym> tag is applied:

bq. *a phrase* → <strong>a phrase</strong> → *a phrase*

If the text is not meant to have *structural* emphasis, but simply to be styled in **bold** (e.g., as a design element) use double asterisks:

bq. ==**a phrase**== → <b>a phrase</b> → **a phrase**

<small>NB: quick tags must open and close within a single paragraph to produce valid markup.</small>

"More information(strong reference at htmlhelp.com)":https://www.htmlhelp.com/reference/html40/phrase/strong.html'

'attributes'
'Attributes'
'Syntax:

(class) 
(#id) 
(class#id) can be combined
{style}
[language]

*Attributes in phrase elements*

%{color:red}red% → <span style="color:red">red</span>

%[fr]rouge% → <span lang="fr">rouge</span>

_(big)red_ → <em class="big">red</em>

*Attributes in block elements*

p(bob). A paragraph → <p class="bob">A paragraph</p>

p{color:#ddd}. A paragraph → <p style="color:#ddd">A paragraph</p>

p[fr]. A paragraph → <p lang="fr">A paragraph</p>

*Block attribute shorthand*

p<. _align left_ 
p>. _align right_
p=. _centered_
p<>. _justified_
p(., p((.  _left indent 1em, 2em, etc._
p). _right indent 1em_

Examples:

h2()>. _right-aligned header 2, indented 1em both sides_
h3=. _centered header 3_
!>/image.gif! _right-aligned image_
p[no]{color:red}. _A Norse of a different colour_'
<p>Syntax:</p>
<p>(class) <br />
(#id) <br />
(class#id) can be combined<br />
{style}<br />
[language]</p>
<p><strong>Attributes in phrase elements</strong></p>
<p><span style="color:red;">red</span> → <span style="color:red">red</span></p>
<p><span lang="fr">rouge</span> → <span lang="fr">rouge</span></p>
<p><em class="big">red</em> → <em class="big">red</em></p>
<p><strong>Attributes in block elements</strong></p>
<p class="bob">A paragraph → <p class="bob">A paragraph</p></p>
<p style="color:#ddd;">A paragraph → <p style="color:#ddd">A paragraph</p></p>
<p lang="fr">A paragraph → <p lang="fr">A paragraph</p></p>
<p><strong>Block attribute shorthand</strong></p>
<p style="text-align:left;"><em>align left</em> <br />
p&#62;. <em>align right</em><br />
p=. <em>centered</em><br />
p&#60;&#62;. <em>justified</em><br />
p(., p((.  <em>left indent 1em, 2em, etc.</em><br />
p). <em>right indent 1em</em></p>
<p>Examples:</p>

	<h2 style="padding-left:1em;padding-right:1em;text-align:right;"><em>right-aligned header 2, indented 1em both sides</em>
h3=. <em>centered header 3</em>
<img src="/image.gif" align="right" alt="" /> <em>right-aligned image</em>
p[no]{color:red}. <em>A Norse of a different colour</em></h2>

'attach_titles_to_permalinks'
'Preferences: Attach titles to permalinks?'
'This setting will attach a dirified version of your article-title to your url, it is either generated automatically or can be manually overridden in the "Advanced Options" when editing an article.

This setting will only have an effect when you are using clean urls. For certain languages this may result in long and ugly urls, in which case you probably want to set this to off.'
<p>This setting will attach a dirified version of your article-title to your url, it is either generated automatically or can be manually overridden in the &quot;Advanced Options&quot; when editing an article.</p>
<p>This setting will only have an effect when you are using clean urls. For certain languages this may result in long and ugly urls, in which case you probably want to set this to off.</p>

'article_category'

'Unlike "sections":https://www.textpattern.com/help/?item=section_category, which define the navigational structure of a site, article categories allow organisation of articles by their subject matter. * raw ingredients
* shopping
* recipes
* presentation
* restaurants
* chefs'

		<li>chefs</li>

* can manage articles, comments and links

* can manage sections and categories
* can manage pages, forms and style
* can view diagnostic information
* can manage site preferences
* can view users
* can manage visitor logs
* can manage plugins
* can import data

h3. Copy Editor

* can manage articles with some limitation:
** cannot delete those belonging to other users
* can manage comments and links
* can manage images and files
* can manage sections and categories
* can manage pages and forms
* can view users
* can manage visitor logs


h3. Staff Writer

* can manage own articles, view other users' articles
* can manage images and files

h3. Freelancer

* can manage own articles in a limited sense: 
** can only modify article until is published
** can apply status of _only_ Draft, Hidden or Pending
* can view other users' articles

h3. Designer

** can only modify article until is published
** can apply status of _only_ Draft, Hidden or Pending
* can view other users' articles
* can manage images and files
* can manage sections
* can manage pages, forms and style'

'title_no_widow'
'Prevent widowed words in article titles'
'The term "widowed word" refers to the single final word of an article title that is shown on a new line when the article title is too long to fit on one line. Widows are considered sloppy typography and should be avoided. Setting this preference to 'yes' will ensure that long article lines will never have only the last word on a separate line. Instead, the two last words will appear on a separate line.
'
<p>The term &quot;widowed word&quot; refers to the single final word of an article title that is shown on a new line when the article title is too long to fit on one line. Widows are considered sloppy typography and should be avoided. Setting this preference to &apos;yes&apos; will ensure that long article lines will never have only the last word on a separate line. Instead, the two last words will appear on a separate line.</p>

'lastmod_keepalive'
'Preferences: Compensate for persistent connection mod_deflate bug?'
'Some versions of the Apache @mod_deflate@ module have a bug that breaks subsequent requests when @Keep-Alive@ is used. Dropping the connection is the only reliable way to fix this. Setting this preference to 'Yes' will close the connection, forcing the browser to reconnect for the next request.'
<p>Some versions of the Apache <code>mod_deflate</code> module have a bug that breaks subsequent requests when <code>Keep-Alive</code> is used. Dropping the connection is the only reliable way to fix this. Setting this preference to &apos;Yes&apos; will close the connection, forcing the browser to reconnect for the next request.</p>

'lastmod_keepalive'

'smtp_from'
'Preferences: SMTP envelope sender address'
'This preference should be left blank unless you experience problems with sending email in Textpattern (if no email is sent at all). Should that problem arise, you can enter any valid email address here, although preferably one that has the same domain name as the website where you've installed Textpattern (i.e. if your website is https://example.com, you might use 'you@example.com').

For those interested in the technical details:

* On UNIX/Mac OS servers, the entered email address is used as the 4th parameter (prefixed with "-f") for the PHP @mail()@ function
* On Windows servers, the entered email address is used to set (or override) the @sendmail_from@ php.ini setting

This ensures that the SMTP envelope sender address (which is not necessarily the same as the "From:" header you normally see in emails) is set to the email address you've entered here, which is a requirement for sending email on some web servers.'
<p>This preference should be left blank unless you experience problems with sending email in Textpattern (if no email is sent at all). Should that problem arise, you can enter any valid email address here, although preferably one that has the same domain name as the website where you&apos;ve installed Textpattern (i.e. if your website is https://example.com, you might use &apos;you@example.com&apos;).</p>
<p>For those interested in the technical details:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>On <span class="caps">UNIX</span>/Mac OS servers, the entered email address is used as the 4th parameter (prefixed with &quot;-f&quot;) for the <span class="caps">PHP</span> <code>mail()</code> function</li>
		<li>On Windows servers, the entered email address is used to set (or override) the <code>sendmail_from</code> php.ini setting</li>
	</ul>

<p>This ensures that the <span class="caps">SMTP</span> envelope sender address (which is not necessarily the same as the &quot;From:&quot; header you normally see in emails) is set to the email address you&apos;ve entered here, which is a requirement for sending email on some web servers.</p>

On UNIX/Mac servers, the entered email address is used as the 4th parameter (prefixed with "-f") for the PHP mail() function.
On Windows servers, the entered email address is used to set (or override) the "sendmail_from" php.ini setting.
This ensures that the SMTP envelope sender address (which is not necessarily the same as the "From:" header you normally see in emails) is set to the email address you've entered here, which is a requirement for sending email on some web servers.'

'install_plugin'
'plugin'
'Plugins: Install plugin'
'Paste the contents of the plugin text file here, then use 'Upload' to begin installation. Any plugin you upload with the same name as one you have previously installed will be overwritten with the uploaded version.

The plugin text you paste here is *not* the raw PHP code but Base64-encoded text, usually preceded by a header containing plugin information (such as plugin name, version and description, author name, etc).'
<p>Paste the contents of the plugin text file here, then use &apos;Upload&apos; to begin installation. Any plugin you upload with the same name as one you have previously installed will be overwritten with the uploaded version.</p>
<p>The plugin text you paste here is <strong>not</strong> the raw <span class="caps">PHP</span> code but Base64-encoded text, usually preceded by a header containing plugin information (such as plugin name, version and description, author name, etc).</p>

'clean_url_data_failed'
'Diagnostics: Clean URL data failed'
'h1. Diagnostics: Clean URL data failed

It appears that either your site URLs (@https://example.com/section/article@) are currently not working or your server does not support the test procedure to determine if clean URLs work or not; if clean URLs on your site work, you can safely ignore this message.

If clean URLs are _not_ working it may mean either:

* your 'Site URL' setting is incorrect, or the page at that address is not managed by this copy of Textpattern.
* your server requires some changes to @.htaccess@ in order to make clean URLs work.
* your server doesn't support clean URLs at all.

The clean URL test attempts to fetch a page from the web address specified in the 'Site URL' preference in order to check that all clean URL parameters are working correctly. If that URL is a different site, or has been replaced with a static page or similar, the test will fail. Until the issue is resolved you will have to use messy URLs on your site instead (@https://example.com?s=section&id=4@). Ensure the 'Permanent link mode' in Admin Preferences is set accordingly.

Note that using a plugin to put a site in 'maintenance mode' may trigger this warning.'

'definition'
'Textile: Definition lists'
'To make a definition list, place the term in its own paragraph, preceded by @;@. Each definition can then be written on subsequent lines preceded by @:@.

h3. Examples

bc. ; Textpattern
: An open source CMS
: Elegant and simple to use
: Friendly community

Will become:

bc. <dl>
  <dt>Textpattern</dt>
  <dd>An open source CMS</dd>
  <dd>Elegant and simple to use</dd>
  <dd>Friendly community</dd>
</dl>

Which, when rendered by a browser, will appear like this:

; Textpattern
: An open source CMS
: Elegant and simple to use
: Friendly community

Just like bulleted and numeric lists, terms and definitions may be nested by using more than one colon / semicolon:

bc. ; Textpattern
: An open source CMS
: Has many sister sites, for example:
;; Plugins
:: Plugin repository
;; Documentation
:: User guides and docs (wiki)
;; Themes
:: Customise the admin-side or public site with themes
: Elegant and simple to use
: Friendly community

Will become:

bc. <dl>
  <dt>Textpattern</dt>
  <dd>An open source CMS</dd>
  <dd>Has many sister sites, for example:
  <dl>
    <dt>Plugins</dt>
    <dd>Plugin repository</dd>
    <dt>Documentation</dt>
    <dd>User guides and docs (wiki)</dd>
    <dt>Themes</dt>
    <dd>Customise the admin-side or public site with themes</dd>
  </dl>
  </dd>
  <dd>Elegant and simple to use</dd>
  <dd>Friendly community</dd>
</dl>

p(alert-block information). *Note:* Unmodified paragraphs will be wrapped in @<p>@ tags.

<a href="https://textpattern.com/textile-dl-list" target="_blank">More information on definition lists in Textile</a>.

h3. HTML documentation (via Mozilla Developer Network)

<a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/dl" target="_blank">The @<dl>@ HTML element</a>.

<a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/dt" target="_blank">The @<dt>@ HTML element</a>.

<a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/dd" target="_blank">The @<dd>@ HTML element</a>.'

'textpattern_version_update'
'Diagnostics: Textpattern CMS version update'
'There is a newer version of Textpattern CMS available. To take advantage of the latest features and security, it is recommended you upgrade.

Please note that while we strive to make the upgrade experience as seamless and pain-free as possible:

* If only the last digit differs from your current version, the upgrade is likely to be backwards compatible with plugins and themes.
* If the last two digits differ from your current version, the upgrade might require updates to some plugins and/or themes, although it will probably continue to work anyway in a limited capacity.
* If the first digit is different from your current version, the upgrade is likely to require updates to most plugins and themes. If you rely heavily on a particular plugin or theme feature, please check with the author or community members before commencing the upgrade.

If the messages reads "There is a problem trying to connect to the RPC server" it means a new version could not be checked at this time, or the server may be down. Updates are checked for every 24 hours so it is likely that next time you visit the Diagnostics panel, all will be well.'
<p>There is a newer version of Textpattern <span class="caps">CMS</span> available. To take advantage of the latest features and security, it is recommended you upgrade.</p>
<p>Please note that while we strive to make the upgrade experience as seamless and pain-free as possible:</p>

	<ul>
		<li>If only the last digit differs from your current version, the upgrade is likely to be backwards compatible with plugins and themes.</li>
		<li>If the last two digits differ from your current version, the upgrade might require updates to some plugins and/or themes, although it will probably continue to work anyway in a limited capacity.</li>
		<li>If the first digit is different from your current version, the upgrade is likely to require updates to most plugins and themes. If you rely heavily on a particular plugin or theme feature, please check with the author or community members before commencing the upgrade.</li>
	</ul>

<p>If the messages reads &quot;There is a problem trying to connect to the <span class="caps">RPC</span> server&quot; it means a new version could not be checked at this time, or the server may be down. Updates are checked for every 24 hours so it is likely that next time you visit the Diagnostics panel, all will be well.</p>
